Coinbase Bank Gets Full OCC Approval as Ripple Leads Crypto Banking Push

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Coinbase has moved closer to the center of the US financial system after securing full approval for its national trust bank charter. The Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) recorded the charter as effective on November 6. The development gives Coinbase a federally supervised framework for expanding its institutional custody and digital asset services.

The approval completes a process that began with Coinbase’s application in October 2025. The OCC granted conditional approval on April 2, 2026, before recording the charter as consummated. Coinbase can now operate through its national trust company under federal supervision.

Coinbase follows Ripple’s final OCC approval on October 26. Two major crypto companies have now secured national trust bank charters within weeks. Their progress shows how digital asset firms are increasingly seeking access to regulated financial infrastructure rather than operating outside traditional banking frameworks.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

Coinbase’s approval follows a similar regulatory path taken by Ripple. The company received conditional approval for Ripple National Trust Bank in December 2025. Its final approval arrived on October 26, completing the process ahead of Coinbase.

Ripple’s charter has specific implications for its stablecoin business. Ripple National Trust Bank can manage the segregated reserves backing RLUSD. It can also act as collateral trustee and provide fiduciary digital asset custody services.

RLUSD will continue to be issued by Standard Custody & Trust Company, Ripple’s New York-regulated trust company. The new federal charter adds another layer of supervision around its reserve management and institutional services.

Coinbase Bank has a different commercial opportunity. Its national trust company can provide federally supervised custody and fiduciary services to institutional clients. Coinbase previously said the charter would support its Prime custody business and other regulated services.

The growing number of crypto trust bank approvals has not gone unchallenged. The Independent Community Bankers of America (ICBA) filed a lawsuit against the OCC on October 2. The group argues that the regulator exceeded its authority by expanding access to national trust charters.

The banking association claims crypto companies receive federal banking credibility without meeting several requirements imposed on traditional banks. These include FDIC insurance, certain capital and liquidity standards, and Community Reinvestment Act obligations.

🚨 BIG: ICBA Sues OCC Over Crypto Trust Bank ChartersThe Independent Community Bankers of America (ICBA) has sued the OCC, arguing that its rules allowing crypto firms to obtain national trust bank charters exceed the agency’s legal authority and do not impose the same… pic.twitter.com/kJ8fVcXPoj

The lawsuit challenges the OCC’s March 2026 rule and related guidance. It argues that the regulator has stretched the national trust charter beyond its original statutory purpose.

The dispute could have consequences beyond Coinbase and Ripple. A successful challenge could complicate the regulatory pathway for other digital asset companies pursuing similar charters.

However, the OCC’s approach reflects a wider shift in US financial regulation. The agency has increasingly opened the door to crypto custody, payments, and stablecoin-related activities under federal supervision. This shift also creates opportunities for emerging blockchain infrastructure projects.

Coinbase Bank’s approval represents more than another regulatory milestone. It shows that crypto companies are competing for a larger role in the financial infrastructure institutions will use.

Air Algerie VIP Lounge Algiers Airport Review: Strong Furniture Store Vibes

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

For the next stop of my Orient Express adventure via Africa trip, I had the chance to visit the Air Algerie VIP Lounge at Algiers Houari Boumediene International Airport (ALG). I had just landed from Montreal (YUL) in Air Algerie’s A330neo business class, and was connecting to Tunis (TUN) in Air Algerie’s A320 business class (a wet leased flight).

The Air Algerie VIP Lounge Algiers is actually pleasant enough. It’s quirky, as it has a ton of seating, and almost feels more like a furniture store than an airport lounge. But I appreciate that the space isn’t crowded, so it’s a nice enough place to pass some time. The food and drink selection is totally unremarkable and okay, so at least it’s not negatively memorable.

The Algiers Airport transit experience was interesting. Algeria is a country that really doesn’t want tourists (getting a visa is a pain), and Air Algerie doesn’t get much connecting international traffic, so not that many people are in international transit.

Upon landing from Montreal, I followed the signage in the direction of “Flight Connections.” Interestingly, I seemed to be among the only people on my flight with an international connection, as everyone else headed to immigration.

I ended up in the “transit room,” as it’s called. There my boarding pass was checked, and then I was given a form that I had to fill out. A form to transit internationally? Odd, but whatever. I then had to wait for an immigration officer to be ready, but he just seemed to be sitting in his window for several minutes without signaling he was ready, so I just waited.

After about five minutes, it was my turn. Then there was a security check, except the security guy clearly wasn’t quite ready yet for anyone, so he was caught off guard by me, and took a second to set things up.

All-in-all, it was a painless enough transit experience thanks to the lack of crowds, even if it was a bit bureaucratic. The Algiers Airport terminal is really big, and it just felt totally deserted during my early morning visit (when a lot of flights leave, in theory).

Once in the international departures area, it was easy to find the Air Algerie VIP Lounge, as there was signage for it right there. The lounge is in the center of the terminal, up a level, so you can either take the elevator, escalator, or stairs.

Once on the upper level, the entrance to the lounge was right there. I loved the Air Algerie Boeing 767 airplane model at the entrance. I’m not sure if this was intended to be a throwback, or if they just hadn’t bothered getting a new model in the nearly decade since the aircraft type was retired. I have a guess, but… 😉

The Air Algiers VIP Lounge Algiers is open 24/7, or something like that. The hours aren’t actually published, but the idea is that if there are flights, then the lounge should also be open.

The Air Algerie VIP Lounge is the primary lounge at Algiers Airport. So in addition to being open to premium passengers on Air Algerie, it also acts as the contract lounge for premium passengers on EgyptAir, Qatar Airways, Royal Jordanian, Saudia, Tunisair, and Turkish Airlines.

Oddly, the lounge isn’t open to Priority Pass members, despite clearly having the capacity to handle more passengers.

The Air Algerie VIP Lounge Algiers is quite a large facility, especially in relation to demand. The lounge basically consists of one large room, and it “opens up” into the rest of the terminal, as it’s essentially just on top of the main passenger concourse.

As I said above, the only way I can describe this lounge is as having very strong furniture store vibes, as there’s just a sea of couches and chairs, all of which are quite plush. I’ll let the pictures speak mostly for themselves.

The lounge was really quiet during my visit. There were maybe a total of a dozen people in the lounge, and I’d say fewer than half of those were passengers, and the remainder were airport staff having their morning coffee, and helping themselves to the buffet.

The lounge was quite well staffed — there were two staff members responsible for overseeing the buffet, while for reasons I can’t figure out, there were three people at the front desk of the lounge (I don’t think a single other person entered during my hour stay, so that strikes me as an overkill, but…).

The Air Algerie VIP Lounge Algiers has a self-serve food & drink selection.

When it comes to food, the selection included finger sandwiches, whole fruit, veggies, cheese, cold cuts, yogurt, cereal, pastries, etc.

There were also some hot options, none of which looked particularly appealing to me (and I also wondered for how long these items had been sitting around). This included eggs, sausage, potatoes, and veggies.

When it comes to drinks, this is a “dry” lounge (so there’s no alcohol). Options included coffee, tea, soft drinks, and water, all of which guests could help themselves to.

The Air Algerie VIP Lounge Algiers has bathrooms that are located just inside the entrance and to the right. They’re down a hall, right next to the kitchen area.

The men’s room had three sinks, three toilet stalls, and two urinals.

In theory there was also a shower suite, but as you can see below, not really.

The Air Algerie VIP Lounge Algiers was the best part of my Air Algerie experience, which isn’t saying a whole lot. The carrier’s flagship lounge is large, and has an okay selection of food and drinks. In an era where so many lounges are crowded, it’s at least nice to have a quiet space like this.

Admittedly this is otherwise a pretty uninteresting lounge, with few amenities, and a lack of variety when it comes to seating options. I definitely wouldn’t arrive early to use this lounge, but it’s also not a bad place to pass some time.

What do you make of the Air Algerie VIP Lounge Algiers?

Phantom Blade Zero Player Is Making Game’s Best Bosses Playable

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Sam Woods is TheGamer’s Senior Reporter. He’s been in games journalism since 2020 and has undertaken a variety of roles in that time. These have included roles such as SEO Editor, Managing Editor at DualShockers, and Global Head of Gaming, and he's also been seen on the BBC. He was promoted to the Senior Reporter position in 2025 following a series of high-quality reports and exclusives for TheGamer.

Since 2020, Sam has written over 3,000 articles and specializes in telling the stories of the people behind the games. He’s interviewed the biggest game directors, some of the industry’s top talents, told the stories of how indie games came to be, and spoken to players about what really makes them tick. He’s also interviewed some of the world’s biggest musical artists to find out how the medium has inspired their work, and TV and Film actors about their roles in video game adaptations. He even hosted a PAX panel in 2021 and made a documentary in 2024 surrounding the creation of a Disney game.

Alongside this, Sam focuses on breaking news. He got the scoop on Perfect Dark’s cancellation, provided context on some AI drama, and revealed that Lady Dimitrescu’s actress would love to step back into the character’s giant shoes. These articles have been picked up by the biggest gaming sites, including IGN, GameSpot, and PC Gamer. He also loves to dive into the numbers behind the games.

Sam’s journey in games journalism has taken him all over the world. He’s covered Gamescom in Germany, Summer Game Fest in Los Angeles, and Tokyo Game Show in Japan, each on three occasions.

He’s been learning Japanese since 2024 with the intention of identifying and translating Japanese-language news and bringing it to English-language audiences first, something he’s done countless times.

Sam’s first review was 2020’s Battletoads reboot, and he’s since reviewed games in The Legend of Zelda, Super Mario, and Pokemon franchises.

While Phantom Blade Zero is a truly excellent game (scoring a 5/5 in my official review), players have been hard at work doing what they can to improve it in the 10 days since it launched. We've seen full Soulslike transformations, game-changing cheat menus, and the addition of Sun Wukong from Chinese folklore.

It feels like the type of game that's going to get consistent support from its already ginormous player base, with that support continuing over recent times. One player is seemingly on a mission to make the game's best bosses into playable characters, with three already completed and ready to go.

Over on Nexus Mods, creator QOutlaw has been creating playable versions of some of Phantom Blade Zero's most memorable bosses. They've made three so far, and they're all excellent.

The first they released was a playable version of Bai Chilian, including her outfits and voice. It's received over 23,000 downloads so far.

Bai Chilian is arguably the best boss from Phantom Blade Zero's second area, Crimson Forest. She has two phases: the first is a more grounded fight where she summons ribbon snakes to aid her in battle. In the second, she becomes possessed, attacking from strings around the arena. If you collect all of the memory flowers related to her, you are assisted in battle by one of Soul's allies. It's a great fight, and while you'll be playing as her base form in this mod, equipping the White Serpent and Crimson Viper swords will make it a lot of fun.

The second mod QOutlaw uploaded is a playable Yuding- No.12 Crescent Blade Puppet. This fight happens late in Phantom Blade Zero and has some of the best animations in the entire game. She also blesses you with what I believe is the best weapon of the game's 30. Probably not as iconic as Bai Chilian, but a great option.

The final mod turns Soul into Mo Yuan, the masked man found in the Loong challenges, played by Donnie Yen. He's a pivotal character in Phantom Blade Zero's overarching story and is vital to the game's true ending. He's also exceptionally cool. And he's Donnie Yen.

I love the work that modders do, and installing any of these will make future NG+ runs of Phantom Blade Zero feel entirely different. A big recommendation from me.

The Midterms Were a Pretty Good Sign for the Right-to-Repair Movement

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Right-to-repair policies weren’t the headline issue in the midterms, but lawmakers across party lines ran on fixing the systems that lock buyers out.

Iowa senator-elect Josh Turek and presumptive Minnesota state representative Jake Johnson both centered repair policies in their platforms, focusing on issues that affect farmers and their equipment. (As of publication, Johnson’s race has not yet been called and may be headed to a recount.) In St. Paul, Minnesota, voters also passed a measure ensuring renters' right to repair with overwhelming support.

The right-to-repair movement, propelled by advocates in the US and internationally, pushes lawmakers for rules that prevent manufacturers from locking owners out of their own devices. Repair efforts tend to be bipartisan, with support ranging from consumers to farmers to wheelchair users.

“A political climate where people want policies that stand up for the little guy, help reduce unnecessary costs, and work in a bipartisan way—that’s a very good environment for right-to-repair,” says Nathan Proctor, senior director of PIRG’s right-to-repair campaign, in a text to WIRED.

Advancing right-to-repair legislation would be a step toward the larger ownership battles taking place in the US. People are angry that companies are turning everything into a subscription service or using software locks to restrict people from modifying or fixing their gear. That meshes with a long-simmering fury about the broader political climate, where tariffs, a memory shortage, and an extremely unpopular war with Iran have driven prices up on just about everything.

As Kevin O’Reilly, executive director of Fulu, an advocacy group focused on consumer digital ownership and product rights, points out, the efforts to claw back control over devices mimic the broader message voters sent at the ballot box.

“There’s a lot to be excited about,” O’Reilly tells WIRED. “People want things to be more affordable, politics to be less polarized, and Congress to solve problems that affect them.”

That discontent points straight to the root of the problem: Section 1201 of the Digital Millennium Copyright Act (DMCA). The 1998 federal statute made it illegal to circumvent digital locks, enabling companies to punish people who break them with possible prison time. Repair advocates have widely decried the legislation, as companies have used it to prevent people from fixing their devices without software access.

For advocates, the reshuffled Congress is the cleanest legislative target in decades. With voter frustration cutting cleanly across party lines, O'Reilly suggests the mandate is hard to ignore.

“If I were a new or returning member of Congress,” O’Reilly says, “I would see DMCA reform as a no-brainer.”

EBBA Architects transforms former agricultural barn in Cornwall into a "calm sanctuary"

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Photography by Rikard Kahn and Benni Allan

London studio EBBA Architects has updated and extended a house within a converted barn in Cornwall, stripping away previous alterations to create open, pared-back interiors.

Topped by a distinctive gambrel roof, the former agricultural barn had already been converted into a dwelling, but also undergone years of incremental, ill-suited adaptations and extensions.

EBBA Architects looked to restore the Dutch barn‘s original, open spatial character, expanding its area by almost a third with the addition of a pavilion-like extension that is framed by columns of white stone.

“The overall concept was about finding clarity in the form of the building and finding ways to add in a way that didn’t take away from the original character,” EBBA’s founding director, Benni Allan told Dezeen.

“Over the years the barn had gathered a series of piecemeal alterations that took away from the original simplicity. Our process involved removing later additions and then adding where felt most appropriate,” he added.

“The house is a place that will act as a place to enjoy the vast contrast of weather throughout the year, while also providing a calm sanctuary and background for family life.”

The ground-floor plan of Dutch Barn is based on the central position of an existing stair, around which a bathroom and storage core has been built to separate the more open kitchen and living spaces.

A row of full-height openings links the existing barn to its extension, where a long, uninterrupted space containing a skylit dining area and lounge opens out onto a covered colonnade via sliding doors.

“The intention is for the house to be used by the large family, and for there to be a blend between spaces,” Allan explained.

“We are always interested in making flexible arrangements in planform, to accommodate different ways of living.”

The home’s interior finishes were also guided by ideas of simplification, with plaster walls, concrete floors and gridded white-painted timber ceilings.

EBBA Architects wanted to emphasise material junctions, as seen in a new oak column in the kitchen, which has been rotated to highlight where it meets the flitch plates that connect it to the exposed ceiling beams.

Externally, this theme was extended into the design of the extension’s roof, which stops short of the edge of the stone columns to emphasise the point where the two meet.

The existing barn was clad in reused cedar shingles that were replaced where necessary, while the extension was built using white stone masonry that references the walls on the adjacent street.

“At first glance the house presents itself as a simple barn, but we like that a series of quite serious architectural moves have been complemented with some playful elements, such as the exaggeration of the junctions and the contrast between textured columns and clear, almost gallery-like floors,” explained Allan.

“We wanted the projecting roof and new columns to read as an independent object that reaches out into the garden and takes up the change in level, rather than as a continuation of the barn,” he added.

EBBA Architects has previously completed a cafe in Marylebone with clay plaster walls and an illuminated glass ceiling, and an apartment in London for its founder that is lined with Douglas fir.

Photography by Rikard Kahn and Benni Allan

Trump Says He Knows 'Nothing' About World Liberty Financial as He Defends UAE Ties

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Trump Says He Knows 'Nothing' About World Liberty Financial as He Defends UAE Ties

U.S. President Donald Trump has defended his family's stake in crypto venture World Liberty Financial, saying he knows "nothing" about the firm despite being a co-owner, in an interview after last week's midterm elections.

Asked by columnist Ross Douthat why his co-ownership of a company partly owned by investors linked to the United Arab Emirates government was different from the kind of arrangement he would call corrupt if it involved the Biden family, Trump replied: "Here's the difference [... ] I'm gonna tell you why. Because I know nothing about it," according to a transcript published by CBS News.

Pressed on why he did not simply divest, Trump said most of his money is managed by Wall Street firms through index funds, and that his sons run the family business. "It's hard to say to them, 'Don't do anything anymore for four years because I'm president,'" he said.

When Douthat asked whether foreign investors in the company believe they are buying influence, Trump said: "I don't know." He argued that conflicts of interest have dogged presidents "going back to George Washington," adding that past presidents "own something, whatever crypto was at the time."

"There's crypto, and before that there was oil," Trump said, adding that, "every president has had this problem."

"I'm a bigger name than other presidents, whether we like it or not," Trump said.

World Liberty Financial, which Eric Trump announced in August 2024, issues the WLFI governance token and the USD1 stablecoin. In January, it launched World Liberty Markets, a lending and borrowing platform built on DeFi protocol Dolomite.

Its UAE links have drawn scrutiny from Democrats. Aryam Investment, a UAE-backed entity tied to the country's national security adviser, Sheikh Tahnoon bin Zayed, agreed to buy a 49% stake in World Liberty Financial for $500 million four days before Trump's January 2025 inauguration, Decrypt reported in February, citing The Wall Street Journal. The deal, signed by Eric Trump on World Liberty's behalf, reportedly preceded the administration's reversal of restrictions on advanced AI chip sales to the UAE. A World Liberty spokesperson said at the time that neither Trump nor his Middle East envoy, Steve Witkoff, had "any involvement whatsoever" in the transaction, ABC News reported. Sen. Chris Murphy (D-CT) described the arrangement in February as "potentially criminal conduct." Trump has previously said he was not aware of the deal.

The company's UAE ties have also surfaced in its legal battle with Tron founder Justin Sun. Sun's lawyers asked a federal court to sanction World Liberty, alleging the firm delayed disclosing UAE ownership that could defeat federal jurisdiction over his lawsuit. World Liberty has called Sun's legal campaign a "meritless PR stunt."

The Trump family's financial stake in the venture is set out in World Liberty's own disclosures. DT Marks DEFI LLC, an entity affiliated with Trump and certain family members, owns about 38% of the holding company behind World Liberty Financial, holds 22.5 billion WLFI tokens, and is entitled to 75% of the net proceeds from WLFI token sales, according to the company. A separate Trump-affiliated entity has an indirect claim on interest earned on the reserves backing USD1. The disclosures state that no member of the Trump family is an officer, director or employee of World Liberty, though Trump's sons have remained involved with the company, Decrypt reported earlier this year.

Ahead of The Witcher 3: Songs of the Past, here's a "lore friendly unofficial expansion to Geralt's life in Toussaint" from a well-established quest modder

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

While its remastered upgrade might have broken cover in late September, The Witcher 3's extra expansion isn't set to join it until next year. With 2027 still a month and a bit away, a modder's taken it upon themselves to add a few more adventures to Geralt's time in Toussaint, Blood and Wine's gorgeous French-ish duchy, before the bloke from the river heads off to Letten for Songs of the Past.

So far, only the opening chapter of the mod - Annals of the Duchy - has been released. It adds in a new quest about Geralt, with help from either Yen or Triss if either have made the trip out to Toussaint as part of your playthrough, getting into a scrape thanks to a halfling who seems to promise Gerry the chance to juice up his vineyard's vines.

The mod's creator, Jontej, has previously put together a couple of other custom Witcher 3 quests. While they do look to have used AI voices in English versions of those mods, for Annals of the Duchy they've enlisted a cast of human actors for both Enlish and Polish versions.

Jontej has plans to expand this new questline going forwards, having cited specific "plans for an extra storyline involving Syanna", whom you might remember from Blood and Wine's main quest. Given that - spoilers ahead - the mod relies on you having gotten the Blood and Wine ending which sees both Anna Henrietta and Syanna survive, as well as having paid to renovate the gardens of Geralt's vineyard. They add that, when it comes to their additions, the "timeline fits quite well [with] the upcoming official Songs of the Past Expansion, which has been confirmed to take place after the events of the main game/Blood and Wine".

As much as I've argued that, as of right now, I struggle to see how Songs of the Past will manage to outdo Blood and Wine as a satisfying end to Geralt's tale in the Witcher game trilogy, I am intrigued by the idea of a few more modded adventures showing Gerry adjusting to the semi-retired life. After all, Toussaint's got to have a few more dungeons tucked away that're worth temporarily dusting off the White Wolf's twin swords for.

To kick off Annals of the Duchy, you'll need to trigger its first quest by taking a stroll from the Corvo Bianco manor house down to its gardens between 11am and 1pm. It's worth noting that any other mod which messes with Yen, Triss or companions in general has a chance to muck things up as far as Jontej's creation goes.

WSL talking points: Tullis-Joyce takes charge and is Shaw the perfect striker?

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The City striker hit a WSL hat-trick for the ninth time while Eva Olid’s United have found defensive stability

Not only did Khadija “Bunny” Shaw score a record-extending ninth WSL hat-trick, she scored a “perfect hat-trick” for the fourth time in the competition, something nobody else has managed more than once. Against West Ham the Jamaica striker opened the scoring with her left foot, headed home her second off the underside of the crossbar and tucked in her third with her right foot late on. It helped her side towards a 5-0 win that opened up a two-point gap at the top of the table and stretched Shaw’s lead in the race to be the top division’s top scorer to a five-goal advantage over her nearest challenger, Océane Hurtré. Can anybody stop her relentless pursuit of another Golden Boot? It would appear not. Tom Garry

Are Liverpool getting the points that their performances deserve? Arguably not, on Sunday’s showing. A 2-1 defeat against Arsenal left Gareth Taylor’s side just two places above the automatic relegation zone and just one spot above the relegation playoff place – despite creating plenty of chances and striking the crossbar, as they impressed in possession. Their defender Grace Fisk feels they are on the right path, saying: “Between both boxes I think we’re one of the best in the league, hands down. Sometimes, I’m watching the way our midfield is playing and the way they move, it’s just a joy to watch.” She added: “We gave it a good fight, I hope the fans see that, but it just wasn’t to be.” TG

London City Lionesses kept pace with Manchester City and Chelsea in the race for the WSL title ahead of a huge opportunity to gain ground next week. The Bromley-based side cruised to a 4-1 win against Birmingham on Sunday, keeping them third while moving them four points behind Chelsea, who face the leaders and title rivals City next week. One or both of the two leading sides will drop points at Stamford Bridge, allowing London City to gain ground in the race – if they beat struggling Arsenal at the Emirates Stadium. On the prospect of closing in on their rivals, the Lionesses head coach, Eder Maestre, said: “First we need to have a good result against Arsenal. The Emirates is going to be a very challenging environment so hopefully we can have a very good result and close the gap.” Emillia Hawkins

Manchester United may have a negative goal difference, having scored 10 and conceded 11 in nine WSL games, but they seem to have found the defensive solidity they were missing at the start of the season. Eight of those 11 goals conceded came in their opening three games as Eva Olid’s side adapted to the tactical demands and style of the new manager. “At the start, we didn’t have time together, just three or four weeks, and now we have more time together, I can feel the team improving,” the manager said after the 1-0 win over Aston Villa. “Still a lot of things to keep working on, not everything is perfect, but it’s good to see the way how things are progressing.” The Red Devils have kept three clean sheets in their past five WSL games and against Villa the backline and goalkeeper Phallon Tullis-Joyce were critical. “The opposition had two big chances and Phallon always helps,” said Olid. “I think that we need more confidence. I feel that the confidence is still a little low and it’s something we need to dedicate a lot of time to improve.” Suzanne Wrack

The result may have been crushing for Charlton, who are still yet to pick up a point in the WSL, but there were rare positives for Karen Hills to take from their 3-2 loss to Crystal Palace. It was the first time they had scored twice in a game this campaign and they accrued twice as many shots on target, six to three, than the home side. “At the end of the day, we’re all in this together. We’re all learning,” said Hills after Bethany England’s 90th-minute winner. “We’re all in a new league, playing against new opposition. But we knew Crystal Palace, we know them really, really well, and that game today was us. We played like a Charlton team does and I thought the performance deserved more.” Charlton have proved they can score at this level. Now they need better defensive performances if they are to earn their first points. SW

Scientists May Have Discovered a Massive Starless Galaxy

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Some galaxies appear to contain all the ingredients needed to make stars, yet have failed to produce almost any.

Scientists have puzzled over why for years. Recent findings suggest that these “dark galaxies”—that is, galaxies that contain gas and dark matter but few or no stars—may be lurking throughout the cosmos. Finding them could help astronomers understand why some galaxies form stars while others remain dark, and test the best models of galaxy formation.

Researchers’ most successful model of the cosmos, known as Lambda-CDM, assumes the existence of two invisible ingredients: dark energy, the mysterious property thought to be driving the accelerating expansion of the universe, as well as cold dark matter, a hypothetical substance that physicists think makes up about 85 percent of the universe’s matter.

Clumps of dark matter, called haloes, provide the gravitational scaffolding for galaxies to form and grow, according to the model. Gas collects inside these haloes, eventually cooling and collapsing to form stars. But astronomers are still trying to understand why this process appears to happen in some haloes but not others.

One possibility is that some contain vast quantities of hydrogen gas, yet form very few stars. That would make them extremely difficult to spot.

Now, researchers have found a promising candidate for such a dark galaxy and published their findings in the Monthly Notices of the Royal Astronomical Society. The object, known as AGC 322753, contains roughly 2 billion times the sun's mass in hydrogen, yet appears to have no stars, though the researchers note that it is too big to be totally starless. However, if confirmed, it would be an unusually massive example of a starless galaxy.

Researchers at Swinburne University of Technology in Australia made the discovery by comparing observations from two existing radio surveys, one conducted by the Arecibo Observatory in Puerto Rico and the other by China's Five-Hundred-Meter Aperture Spherical Radio Telescope. Both telescopes had picked up a massive cloud of hydrogen.

Although other researchers have identified several dark galaxy candidates over the past decade, this one stands out for another reason: It may be rotating.

Hydrogen gas emits radio waves at a signature wavelength, which shortens when the gas moves toward Earth and stretches when it moves away. The signal from AGC 322753 shows two distinct peaks, suggesting that one side of the gas cloud is moving towards us while the other is receding.

This could mean that the gas has settled into an organized, rotating structure, much like the discs in ordinary galaxies, despite apparently having formed no stars.

"The theoretical models and the simulations show us that dark galaxies shouldn't rotate, so this is extremely interesting," says Marco Monaci, an author of the study at Swinburne University of Technology.

If AGC 322753 really is a rotating disc with no stars, astronomers will have to explain how the gas organized into such a structure without producing them.

That isn’t the only puzzle. Most models of galaxy formation predict that a cloud holding this much hydrogen should have formed at least a few stars, as its densest patches collapse under their own gravity and ignite.

The paper’s coauthors suggest it may be very unlikely that AGC 322753 contains absolutely no stars. So, instead, they think it may contain stars too faint to see–an “almost dark” galaxy. If so, they must be very faint indeed, because even deep optical images from the DESI Legacy Imaging Surveys, which search for starlight rather than the hydrogen gas detected by radio telescopes, reveal none.

From this, the team estimates that AGC322753 contains at least 40 times more mass in hydrogen than in stars, raising questions about what exactly suppressed stars from forming.

But further observations, such as ones made by the Very Large Array, a network of radio antennas in New Mexico, will be needed to confirm whether AGC 322573 is truly a dark—or almost dark—galaxy.

"Finding out more about it really is an essential next step at the moment. It's intriguing, and I want to know more," says Robert Minchin at the National Radio Astronomy Observatory.

Minchin knows how misleading such signals can be. He was involved in the discovery of VIRGOHI21, a dark galaxy candidate found in the early 2000s that was later reinterpreted as a long stream of hydrogen gas pulled from a nearby galaxy by gravity, known as a tidal tail.

Gas torn away like this can keep some of its original motion and mimic the signature of rotation, so Minchin says more observations are needed to rule out the same explanation for AGC 322753. Yet there is no obvious nearby galaxy it could have come from.

Rhys Taylor, an astronomer at the Astronomical Institute of the Czech Academy of Sciences in Prague, says tidal debris is unlikely to explain the observations. Reproducing the candidate dark galaxy’s distinctive double-peaked profile through such a process “is technically possible, but it’s fantastically unlikely,” Taylor says.

Taylor and Minchin both flag another concern, though. The two radio surveys that the researchers used placed the hydrogen at noticeably different positions in the sky. That suggests AGC 322753 may be an extended cloud of gas rather than a compact galaxy, another possibility that follow-up observations could help untangle.

Whether AGC 322753 turns out to be completely dark or merely exceptionally faint, understanding objects like it could still help astronomers work out why some galaxies form stars while others apparently do not, says Elisabeth Adams at the Planetary Science Institute.

One possible reason is a galaxy that spins so fast that its gas stays too spread out to collapse. Another is that ultraviolet light from the universe’s earliest stars heated the gas, keeping it too warm to condense. Studying such almost-dark galaxies could help tell these phenomena apart, revealing how the conditions of the early universe shaped the galaxies we see today.

“Once it’s no longer a dark galaxy, people seem to stop caring, and I actually think that’s not the right approach,” says Adams.

But if AGC 322753 truly is starless, it would be one of the most massive dark galaxies ever identified, potentially helping explain where the missing galaxies predicted by Lambda-CDM have been hiding. It could even help astronomers understand why some gas-rich galaxies form stars while others don't, providing a valuable test for models of galaxy formation—and perhaps even offering new clues about the nature of dark matter itself.

Have Today’s Strength Machines Finally Caught Up with Free Weights for Muscle Growth?

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Sign up below to receive our newest workout routines, recipes, news stories, and offers from our partners

With the right plan and the right discipline, you can get seriously shredded in just 28 days.

At age 62, "Big Bill" shares his wisdom to dominate one of the ultimate strength marks.

Follow these fit women we're crushing on for inspiration, workout ideas, and motivation.

Since the invention of the barbell, free weights have long been considered the best for building muscle and strength. Machines? Those were for beginners, rehab, or finishing off a muscle after the real work.

Today, the barbell’s crown might be slipping.

Many well-equipped commercial gyms now have Pendulum squats, Arsenal Strength rows, converging chest presses, and other machines that look like they belong in a NASA training facility.

Modern designs feature sophisticated resistance curves and adjustable movement paths, letting lifters challenge their muscles without worrying about anything but the lift.

But before you put down the barbell, consider this question.

Are modern strength machines better for building muscle, or are we mistaking clever engineering for superior results?

They’re effective, versatile, and have helped generations of lifters build impressive muscle and strength.

“Free weights should always remain the foundation,” explains Matt Wenning of Wenning Strength. “They develop strength, coordination, and stability that machines cannot fully replicate.”

Here are three other reasons why they still work.

Barbells are easy to load, and most dumbbells increase in 2.5- to 5-pound increments. This makes it easy to track progress. Barbells let you handle heavier loads because they’re more stable than dumbbells.

Our bodies include prime mover muscles like the quads and smaller stabilizer muscles like the rotator cuff. Free weights engage both, but machines require less stabilization because they’re more stable. Stabilization helps build strength and coordination, but more stabilization doesn’t always mean more muscle.

Many machines have a fixed range of motion, but with dumbbells, you’re not locked into a fixed lifting path. This lets you adjust grip angles, elbow position, and pressing or pulling paths. That freedom lets you adjust to your needs and comfort.

Jay Ashman of Ashman Strength has a unique take on the rise of the machines.

“We are in the midst of the “optimization” trend, that’s heavy on aesthetics,” he explains. “Why would a trainee not use the easiest tool for the job? Doesn’t make it the best tool, but it’s the most efficient.”

Companies like Arsenal Strength, Rogers Athletic (Pendulum Strength), and Prime Fitness have taken advantage of that with more ways to customize exercises to your body.

A big development is how they apply resistance throughout an exercise’s range of motion. They do this with cams, lever arms, and adjustable loading positions. For example, some Prime Fitness machines offer multiple loading positions that let lifters emphasize different parts of the range of motion.

Why does this matter? Your muscles don’t produce equal force at every joint angle. A machine that places resistance where a muscle can produce more force may give you a muscle-building advantage.

Greater stability isn’t new, but it’s still worth mentioning. When you lift along a fixed path, without worrying about stabilizing, your target muscles get more love while your stabilizers take a break.

“If I’m trying to maximize mechanical tension through a particular muscle,” explains Gareth Sapstead, CSCS. “Reducing the stability demands can be a good thing because you’re challenging the target muscle rather than worrying about balance, coordination, or maintaining position.”

That’s handy when hypertrophy is the goal.

Adjustable Movement Paths and Better Ergonomics

The modern machines offer more ways to adjust your setup. Adjustable seats, chest pads, starting positions, handles, and range-of-motion settings help lifters find positions that better match their bodies. The newer chest press machines feature converging arms, allowing your hands to move closer together as you approach lockout. Some row machines use diverging movement paths, while others allow the arms to move independently.

Independent Loading and Unilateral Training

Another feature of many modern machines is independently moving arms.

Instead of both arms working together as a unit, each side moves separately. This lets lifters perform unilateral reps, notice side-to-side differences, and adjust their training if one side is weaker. Unilateral training isn’t new, but modern machines combine single-limb movement with stability and a guided path while reducing coordination demands.

Is one really better than the other for building strength and muscle? “We know that our muscles, “ explains Ashman. “We cannot tell the difference between free weights and machines regarding muscle growth.”

A 2023 systematic review and meta-analysis examined 13 studies involving 1,016 people comparing free-weight and machine training. Five of those studies measured hypertrophy, and the researchers found that neither had the advantage.

Another systematic review and meta-analysis by Heidel and colleagues concluded that machines and free weights both built similar amounts of muscle. A separate 2023 study comparing free-weight and machine-based exercises found similar improvements in muscle hypertrophy and strength between training approaches.

NOTE: These studies compare general categories of machines and free weights, not the newest machines featuring adjustable resistance profiles.

Although muscle growth appears the same, the strength findings reveal something interesting.

The Haugen meta-analysis found that lifters who trained with free weights improved more when tested with free weights. Those who trained with machines also performed better on machine-based strength tests. Strength is equipment-specific, with limited crossover in technique. For example, a Pendulum squat develops the quads and glutes, but it won’t teach the same balance, coordination, or barbell-specific technique as a barbell back squat.

You’ve seen that science can’t separate them. But with all the money they’ve poured into development, they must have the muscle-building edge.

Let’s compare the Pendulum Squat to the barbell squat to see where the modern machine might offer an advantage.

The Pendulum squat uses a guided, arcing motion that reduces balance demands. Depending on the machine’s design and your setup, this can make it easier to achieve depth and focus on leg drive. Some Pendulum machines also feature resistance profiles that adjust how challenging different portions of the movement feel.

The Machine advantage: Greater external stability allows some lifters to challenge their quads more without balance, bar positioning, or torso control becoming the limiting factor. For lifters with mobility limitations or discomfort supporting a barbell, the Pendulum Squat machine offers a viable alternative.

The barbell advantage: The back squat develops barbell-specific strength, coordination, and the ability to control heavy loads. It’s also essential for competitive powerlifters and more versatile for developing lower-body strength.

The verdict: If your goal is bigger quads, the Pendulum squat may be a better fit, but it doesn’t guarantee greater muscle growth. For more total-body strength and muscle, the barbell is still your best bet.

Modern machines let you focus on specific muscles and ranges of motion, and you can adjust the setup to suit your needs, but strength training is more than just building muscle.

Maximal Strength and Exercise Specificity

If your goal is to improve maximal strength, the barbell is still the way to go. No amount of Pendulum squats will fully replace the skill, coordination, and technique required to get under a heavy bar and squat. Barbell squats also improve your ability to coordinate muscle actions, stabilize the load, and produce force in that movement.

Machines can strengthen the muscles involved, but they don’t reproduce every demand of a free-weight lift.

Free weights require you to control the load rather than allowing the machine to guide it. This means coordinating multiple joints, maintaining your position, and adjusting your movement as the weight travels through space.

“I still favor free weights for developing strength, coordination, positional control,” explains Sapstead. “The ability to produce force through more complex movement patterns works well for clients who want a physique that performs as well as it looks.”

Modern machines reduce some of those demands, but free weights offer a better chance to practice coordination and movement skills under load.

With free weights, you have everything you need for a great workout. They make it easy to modify exercises without changing equipment. Adjust your grip, stance, range of motion, or body position to change the training stimulus.

Modern machines, especially premium plate-loaded equipment, cost more, take up more space, and often serve a specific purpose.

Modern machines have challenged the idea that barbells and dumbbells are superior for building muscle, but free weights are popular for a reason. Free weights remain essential for developing specific strength, offer greater versatility, and demand movements that many machines reduce.

“Free weights have an important place, and I wouldn’t necessarily say one is better than the other,” says Sapstead.

The question isn’t whether machines have dethroned free weights. It’s whether you’re choosing the right equipment for your goals. For most lifters, the answer is both, not either or.

Sapstead’s approach is refreshing. “The best exercise is the one that places sufficient tension on the muscles you’re trying to develop through a useful range of motion while allowing consistent progression,” he says.

“Sometimes that’s a barbell, sometimes a dumbbell, and sometimes a really well-designed machine.”

There’s no need to pick a side, but you do need to choose exercises based on the three factors below.

If your goal is a bigger squat, deadlift, or bench, free weights rule because you need to be under or over the barbell. “Machines should support your free-weight training, not replace it, “ says Wenning.

But building muscle offers you more flexibility between free weights and machines. The important question isn’t whether you use a machine or free weights to build muscle. It’s all about progressive overload. If one is easier for you, go with it.

Use Machines to Reduce Unnecessary Fatigue

Modern machines let you adjust your setup for enhanced stability, so you don’t have to worry about stabilizing or balancing. This doesn’t mean machines eliminate fatigue or that free weights always produce more. It means that choosing exercises with different stability and positioning demands can help distribute fatigue more effectively throughout your workout. Wenning agrees.

“Machines are tools to help improve performance with free weights,” he explains. “They allow us to isolate weaknesses, build additional muscle, and increase training volume without the same level of systemic fatigue.”

Pushing the limits with free weights often requires a spotter. If you want to do a bench-press drop set and you don’t have a spotter, wouldn’t it be better and more efficient to do it on a chest-press machine, where failure doesn’t mean you’re pinned under the barbell?

Sapstead agrees. “ Taking a hack squat or leg extension to failure is generally more manageable than doing the same with a heavy barbell squat.”

So does Ashman. “Training to failure is much safer on a machine than with free weights, and because I’m seeing an emphasis on “effective reps” and training close to failure.”

This workout combines free-weight pressing with supported machine pulling.

This lower-body workout combines free-weight hip-hinge and unilateral exercises with machine-based training.

Now more than ever, machines are neck and neck with free weights in the muscle-building stakes.

With improved resistance profiles, greater stability, and more adjustable lifting paths, today’s machines have earned their place alongside barbells and dumbbells in the weight room.

But have they overtaken free weights for building muscle? Not quite.

Research shows that machines and free weights can produce similar muscle growth, and we don’t yet have convincing evidence that newer machines yield superior results.

But it is not about choosing sides; it’s about choosing which better fits your goals and body type.

7-Minute Morning Stretch Routine for the Flexibility You (Desperately) Need

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Even when you have only seven minutes, you can still complete a practice that releases tension throughout your entire body. The following quick morning stretch routine takes you through an entire sequence of stretch-y yoga poses, focused awareness, and slow breathing.

The following quick yoga routine for your hamstrings, hips, and shoulders can be done in a doable amount of time and will definitely help set the tone for your day and give you that much-needed initial boost of energy. Let’s get started!

You’ll definitely recognize a lot of the poses but there are also some uncommon variations that maximize flexibility. No props are required although you are always welcome to use them.

Kneel on the mat and bring your big toes together, knees apart, and instead of folding straight forward, crawl your hands and fingertips toward the upper left corner of your mat as you fold your chest toward the mat in a version of Child’s Pose. Take 3 big breaths here as you press your hips toward your heels and feel the stretch along your right side body.

Take one more inhale here as you lengthen, and then walk your hands toward the right side of the mat and fold on down. Find lots of length through your left side. One more big inhale through the nose and exhale to let it go. Bring your hands back to center.

Lift onto your hands and knees, palms under your shoulders, knees underneath your hips. Step your right foot in between your hands and keep your knee over your ankle in Low Lunge. As you lift your chest and arms, feel your tailbone lengthen down toward the mat. At the same time, you want to lift out of your lower back so you’re not collapsing there.

Circle your hands to the mat and straighten your right leg. You might need to wiggle your front heel a little farther away from you. Then fold forward over your leg, feeling the stretch in your hamstrings, in Half Splits.

Bend your right knee again. This time, tuck your back toes under, lift your back knee off the mat, and come onto your fingertips into a Runner’s Lunge. Think of pressing your hips down and lifting your heart forward. You might want to rock a little back and forth. Let your hips be heavy, so gravity is doing the work.

Now straighten your front leg any amount and spin your back heel down as if you’re in Pyramid Pose. Keep pressing through both feet. You can shorten your stance if you need in this intense stretch for the hamstrings. See if you can relax your neck and take a breath in through the nose and a long, slow exhalation out the mouth. Stay here for a few more breaths.

Bend into your front knee, plant your hands on the mat, and step back to Downward-Facing Dog. You want your hands to be shoulder-distance apart and your feet hip-distance apart. Bend one knee, straightening the opposite knee and pushing into that heel, and then switch sides.

Inhale as you shift forward into Plank Pose.

Slowly lower all the way down to your belly and take a little backbend here as you lift your chest in Cobra Pose.

Then repeat the previous poses on the other side, including Low Lunge, Half Splits, Runner’s Lunge, Pyramid Pose, and Downward-Facing Dog.

From Down Dog, lean into your left leg, reach your right leg toward the ceiling, bend your right knee, and open up that right hip in Three-Legged Dog. You’re trying to lift your right knee as high as it can possibly go. Then switch sides.

Bring your knees to the mat, sit on your heels, and interlace your fingers behind your lower back. Shrug your shoulders down and away from your ears. Open up as you lift your knuckles away from you.

Stay here or lean forward and lightly bring the crown of your head to the mat and lift your shoulders away from your ears in Rabbit Pose, keeping most of your weight in your knees and feet.

Wherever you are, take a big breath in and then release.

Let’s come to take a seat in any way that is comfortable for you here. Notice how you feel now after just 7 minutes of practice. Stay here for a few long, slow breaths before continuing with your day.

Thank you so much for doing this quick 7-minute morning stretch routine class with me!

Thomas van Tonder Just Crossed Africa’s Highest Bridge, By Hand

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Sign up below to receive our newest workout routines, recipes, news stories, and offers from our partners

With the right plan and the right discipline, you can get seriously shredded in just 28 days.

At age 62, "Big Bill" shares his wisdom to dominate one of the ultimate strength marks.

Follow these fit women we're crushing on for inspiration, workout ideas, and motivation.

If you’ve ever struggled to complete a set of money bars, spare a thought for obstacle course athlete, Thomas van Tonder, who just completed the kind of challenge that you have to see in order to believe it’s true: scaling Africa’s highest bridge—by hand.

Red Bull-sponsored thrill seeker, Thomas van Tonder, 34, and was born in South Africa took on 600 monkey bars that were fixed under the arch of the Bloukrans Bridge, en route to besting the longest set of bars ever built. And, as if this weren’t tough (or insane) enough, van Tonder had to follow the arch of the bridge, forcing him to to deal with an elevation and descent, all while his lactic acid was fit to burst.

At its peak, the bridge stood 216 meters above ground level, with van Tonder taking on 280 meters in distance. But on Oct. 26, 2026, the able athlete finished his perilous journey in 47 minutes and 37 seconds.

Van Tonder took on the most monkey bars, at the highest level, for the longest distance, but there were times when he questioned himself. “From the middle segment to the end, I was unsure if I’d actually make it,” he explained after he got firmly back on land.

To complete the challenge, van Tonder made strategic breaks by resting his legs on the bars, but this presented additional difficulties. “There were many points where I was on the edge of being able to actually get my legs up or not. It was definitely not a given.”

At the finish line, van Tonder was truly spent, and suffered with serious skin loss on his hands, but then making history was never easy. “One of the hardest things I’ve ever done,” he concluded.

Well, we’ll certainly never look at a set of monkey bars the same way again!

Kim Hellberg footage after Middlesbrough's draw v Watford is becoming a talking point

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Middlesbrough dropped points and failed to score for the second time this week on Saturday at Watford, and Boro boss Kim Hellberg was seen visibly frustrated after the final whistle.

The draw at Vicarage Road saw Middlesbrough relinquish the top spot in the Championship ahead of the November international break, with Birmingham City climbing above them on goal difference.

It's not how Boro would have wanted to sign off this mini block of fixtures between international breaks, and the performance against Watford, especially, would have provided plenty of food for thought in the dugout.

Pick any two players. Send the code, and your mate enters it here to play the same pair.

Indeed, this was the second successive game without leading talisman and Championship top scorer Will Lankshear due to injury, and it's almost universally agreed that if the 21-year-old was leading the line on Saturday, they'd have cruised to victory, as Boro had 18 shots, 34 touches in Watford's box and four big chances, but couldn't score.

Middlesbrough boss Kim Hellberg has shown how passionate he is about the game in the 12 months he's spent in charge at the Riverside. Therefore, it was clear that a result like this, following such a dominant performance, was going to affect him.

After the game, the Swede was filmed staring blankly at the ground for around half a minute, just reflecting on the game, and Boro fans have reacted accordingly.

Some have made jokey comments about the state of the frontline when Lankshear is injured. One Boro fan took it upon themselves to caption the video, "What having Kyle Joseph as an attacking option does to man." Another commented, "Please god make Will be fit, please god make Will be fit…..🙏."

"He’s got Kyle Joseph depression," was also among the reactions to the video.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top nine toughest fanbases in the EFL Championship right now from least to most

The majority of comments in response to Hellberg's post-match reaction were about how invested he is in the game, though, and Middlesbrough fans know how lucky they are to have the Swede in charge.

"You can just tell this isn’t performative. Like the fist bumps and the celebrations when we win, or the tears during the play-off debacle last season, Kim ‘gets it’, he’s immersed himself into the #Boro way. Never seen a manager so invested in us," one Boro fan tweeted.

"Carries it all, the highs and lows just like the fans. “A good leader leads the people from above them. A great leader leads the people from within them," was also among the comments, as was, "The reaction of a man who puts the weight of the world on his own shoulders #UTB."

"Very powerful, but also makes me want to give him a hug and tell him he's one of my favourite ever Boro gaffers and everything will be alright," someone commented in response to the video, showcasing the love and respect Boro fans have towards their head coach.

This is the reaction of a man spent after a disappointing result, having coached what was effectively the perfect game.

Plenty of head coaches nowadays are focused on the journey, the performance levels and good underlying numbers, and Hellberg is definitely one of those, but he also recognises how important it is to win games, too.

As one Boro fan mentioned, he carries the weight of expectation amongst Middlesbrough fans, who are desperate to return to the Premier League after a decade away, and you can imagine he takes not winning as seriously as some of those in the stands do, too.

It's reactions like this that are why many are touting Hellberg to become a top-flight manager in the near future, and Boro supporters hope it'll be with them.

However, it's hard to ignore the fact that, while Lankshear sits on the sidelines, Middlesbrough struggles to score goals and win games.

Boro's wasteful nature in front of goal dominated the narrative surrounding them in the final few months of last season as they slipped out of the running for automatic promotion and into the play-offs, and they could have been out of sight in the first half of their play-off semi-final first leg against Southampton if not for poor finishing, too.

Lankshear has papered over those cracks, as he's been brilliant at finishing the plenty of chances Middlesbrough create in a game, but the alarm bells are starting to ring without him.

Do you think Middlesbrough can turn their finishing troubles around without Will Lankshear in the side? Let us know in the comments.

Hopefully, the 21-year-old will be back after the international break and won't suffer any more injury setbacks, as that could be the difference between promotion and some grim de ja vu.

It's something Hellberg will be mulling over for the next two weeks, and it seemingly started immediately after the final whistle at Watford.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

2026 XRP Rich-List Thresholds Fall Sharply From 2020

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Home » Ripple (XRP) » 2026 XRP Rich-List Thresholds Fall Sharply From 2020

The accounts multiplied, adding lower-percentage cut-offs required to land in any fixed-percentage tier.

The amount of XRP tokens needed to rank in the upper tiers of the XRP rich-list has dropped significantly over the past six years.

A comparison of snapshots from July 2020 and early November 2026 shows lower entry points across major percentiles, even as the number of funded accounts has climbed past 8.3 million.

The most commonly cited threshold tells a clear story. In July 2020, an account needed roughly 80,033 XRP to enter the top 1%. By November 6, 2026, that figure had fallen to about 43,313 XRP.

Similar XRP rich-list declines appear at other levels: the top 0.01% entry dropped from around 24.2 million XRP to roughly 3.6 million, while the top 0.1% moved from about 750,000 XRP to nearly 271,000.

Lower tiers followed a very similar pattern. The top 0.5% requirement fell from approximately 150,000 XRP to 78,000, the top 5% from about 10,400 to 7,200, and the top 10% from roughly 3,000 XRP to just over 2,000.

🚨XRP RICH LIST JUST UPDATED!!! 🤑 TOP 4% = 10,000 XRP!🚨Top 1% = 43,301 XRPTop 4% = 10,003 XRPTop 10% = 2,056 XRP Are you $XRP Rich?👁🤑#XRP #Ripple #BTC #Altcoins #Crypto pic.twitter.com/RfIwFPsMOq

Recent live trackers place the top 1% cutoff in a similar range, confirming the broader downward shift as the account base expands.

The decline coincides with steady growth in funded accounts on the XRP Ledger. The network now exceeds 8.3 million accounts, a substantial increase from earlier years.

Adding large numbers of smaller accounts mathematically lowers the balance required to land in any fixed percentage tier, even if existing large holders do not sell.

Most accounts still hold relatively little; the median balance remains in the low tens of XRP, and a large majority sit well below 1,000 XRP.

This XRP rich-list expansion has increased the absolute number of accounts that qualify for higher percentiles. The top 1% now includes tens of thousands more addresses than it did a few years ago, simply because the total base is larger.

Raw account rankings do not map cleanly to individual holders. Many people maintain multiple wallets, and a single exchange address can represent thousands of customers. One observer noted holding roughly a dozen XRP wallets personally.

I have about 10 XRP wallets. Yep, many holders have many so yeah there is that.

Exchange and institutional addresses dominate the very top of the list, so concentration figures that treat every account as a separate person overstate how widely the supply is held by retail.

Ripple-linked accounts and major exchanges continue to control large portions of the supply, including substantial amounts held in escrow.

These structural features mean the falling percentile thresholds reflect both genuine growth in the number of accounts and the persistent weight of large custodial and institutional balances.

The lower entry points make certain ranking milestones more accessible in pure XRP terms than they were in 2020. At the same time, the data does not indicate that large holders have broadly distributed their tokens or that overall concentration at the top has disappeared.

The XRP rich-list comparison offers a clear view of how the XRP Ledger’s account base and balance distribution have changed over six years. It remains a snapshot of addresses, not a direct count of individual investors or a forecast of future price action.

podcast: architecture that refuses signature styles for spaces shaped by instinct

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

the room for utopias podcast continues with indian architects and designers exploring how to dismantle signature styles to create spaces rooted in human instinct.

Challenging the impulse to package buildings into neat aesthetic categories, the Room for Utopias podcast series returns with Indian architects and designers interrogating the tension between enduring human rituals and the myth of signature style. Realized in partnership with INDX|GLOBAL during designboom.paris, this edition, themed A Future Without Formula, investigates what happens when practices step away from recognizable branding. Across two panel debates, hosted by designboom’s Managing Editor, Iñaki Laguardia, the series frames architectural identity not as a static visual stamp, but as an evolving narrative rooted in environmental responsibility and a fluid way of thinking.

The fifth episode of Room for Utopias brings together Ankit Gupta of Ankit Gupta & Ridhima Sogani Architects, Shalini Ganguli of Shalini Ganguli Interiors, and Kshitiz Manu of K Manu & Associates to examine how architecture can balance fast-evolving lifestyles with persistent biological instincts. The conversation moves past superficial trends to identify what built spaces must preserve and what they must urgently reinvent.

Kshitiz Manu of K Manu & Associates distinguishes between daily habits and human nature through a computational metaphor. ‘Ritual is a software and instinct is an operating system. Rituals can be changed… but our instinct stays the same. To maintain that balance is architecture,’ Manu explains. He critiques hermetic, high-tech interiors that disconnect occupants from nature, warning against what he terms ‘2:00 p.m. building’ where static artificial lighting and perpetual climate control freeze the perception of time. Instead, he advocates for spaces that let moving sunlight and lengthening shadows reveal the day’s progression, emphasizing that a building’s real life only begins once the designer departs and daily chaos takes over.

Shalini Ganguli of Shalini Ganguli Interiors addresses domestic fragmentation caused by private screens and self-contained bedrooms, urging designers to safeguard areas of unstructured shared presence. Ganguli argues that rooms must intuitively enable families to coexist without forced interaction. ‘In many rooms you have your own television, you can completely lock yourself out… but what happens is that your life goes unwitnessed. It is our moral responsibility to not ever get rid of areas in the house which enable the family to interact with each other,’ Ganguli states, calling for homes that accommodate human connection and layered memories over sterile, picture-perfect staging.

Ankit Gupta of Ankit Gupta & Ridhima Sogani Architects emphasizes the emotional power of spatial sequencing. Drawing on historic typologies, Gupta describes how traditional architecture moderated privacy through layered transitions, leading occupants progressively from public streets into verandas, central courtyards, and finally secluded quarters. He argues that contemporary luxury lies in this subtle, unhurried progression rather than loud visual statements, requiring an earthy, climate-responsive material palette sensitive to local topography, whether building for the dry heat or colder mountain regions.

The sixth conversation dismantles the commodification of architectural aesthetics. Moderator Iñaki Laguardia sits down with Sonali Bhagwati of Designplus Architects (DPA), Priyanka Mehra of PS Design, Manish Gulati of MOFA Studio, and Ajay Arya of A Square Designs to interrogate whether signature styles limit creative evolution and contextual responsiveness.

Sonali Bhagwati of Designplus Architects (DPA) differentiates between superficial packaging and genuine practice identity. ‘When you look at identity, you are actually looking at the core values that a firm or a practice wants to imbibe. You don’t have to try and superimpose a particular style which is trending onto everything,’ Bhagwati states. For her, design s anchored in a deep narrative rather than aesthetic imitation: ‘The moment your narrative is strong, your architecture will always have a place.’

Manish Gulati of MOFA Studio challenges the historical obsession with stylistic signatures, arguing that styles are retrospective constructs invented by critics to sell architecture. ‘Style is only useful for critics and journalists… It’s the packaging. If you have to sell something and you don’t package it well, it won’t sell,’ Gulati remarks. Describing his studio as’’optimistic non-conformists,’ Gulati asserts that architecture should respond to personal aspirations, user memory, and pressing planetary urgencies rather than dogmatic stylistic rules. ‘Future is not about style. Future is about what is important at the moment  — like climate change — that’s the most important factor when it comes to architecture,’ he notes.

Priyanka Mehra of PS Design defends contextual belonging, balancing client aspirations with geographic rootedness. ‘The house must sit where it belongs. A design has to be rooted in identity, in context where it is sitting,’ Mehra emphasizes. She argues that individual stories should drive spatial concepts. ‘No two individuals are alike. Why do the houses need to be the same?’

Ajay Arya of A Square Designs reflects on the evolving nature of urban living, noting that major cities are experiencing a transitional phase where rigid stylistic dogma is giving way to fluid expression. Arya argues that an architect’s identity should not be bound to reproducing the same look repeatedly, but should instead manifest in how materials are combined and softened to reflect the personality of the inhabitant. ‘I feel creativity should be fluid… and also they should have freedom of thought, and how new things would just come our way in the future,’ Arya shares, emphasizing that spaces must balance modern trends with enduring personal resonance.

From domestic thresholds that allow natural light and time to mark the day, to studios actively unlearning signature aesthetics in pursuit of ecological and public responsibility, both conversations advocate for an architecture unburdened by dogma. These practitioners show that utopias in contemporary practice do not require inventing a novel aesthetic stamp. By prioritizing core values over trending formulas, they point toward an architecture that remains grounded, adaptable, and genuinely alive long after construction ends.

guests: Ankit Gupta (Ankit Gupta & Ridhima Sogani Architects | @ankitguptaarchitects), Shalini Ganguli (Shalini Ganguli Interiors | @shaliniganguliinteriors), Kshitiz Manu (K Manu & Associates | @kshitizmanu), Sonali Bhagwati (Designplus Architects | @dpa_designplusarchitecture), Priyanka Mehra (PS Design | @psdesign.co.in), Manish Gulati (MOFA Studio | @mofa_studio), and Ajay Arya (A Square Designs | @asquaredesigns)

in partnership with: AMICI | @amici_india; MARMO SOLUTIONS | @marmosolutionsindia; AGGLOTECH | @agglotech
