The 13 Best London Museums to Visit

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

London’s numerous museums feature some of the best art, history, science, and cultural collections in the world. With many of these institutions free to enter, paying a visit to one or more can even be an inexpensive day out.

Many of the museums listed here have recently gone through major revamps, and over the coming year there will be even more destinations to visit, with the long-awaited London Museum coming to the historic Smithfield Market and the Museum of Shakespeare opening on the site of the Curtain Playhouse.

If you’re looking for extraordinary cultural objects from the dawn of civilization, head over to the venerable British Museum. There you’ll find such treasures as an impressive collection of ancient Mesopotamian items; the second-century BC Rosetta Stone, which provided the key to deciphering ancient Egyptian hieroglyphics; and the fifth-century BC ancient Greek Parthenon Marbles. Look up to see British starchitect Norman Foster’s amazing glass roof, completed in 2000. The British Museum holds more than eight million objects covering over two million years of human history, with about 80,000 objects on view at any given time. If you feel overwhelmed with the choice of what to see, book a tour and enjoy the luxury of being in the hands of a knowledgeable guide.

Right in the center of London on Trafalgar Square, the National Gallery offers artworks dating from 1260 right up to the 20th century—from Da Vinci to David, Canaletto to Constable, Michelangelo to Monet, Rousseau to Rubens, Cezanne to Seurat, Vermeer to Velazquez to Van Gogh, and that’s just a small fraction of its offerings. And it’s free to enter. For a fee, there are also larger exhibitions in the much acclaimed, recently renovated Sainsbury Wing. The Sainsbury Wing is quite the character itself, of course, ever since in 1984 King Charles III, while still Prince of Wales, described the initial proposed design for it as a “monstrous carbuncle on the face of a much-loved and elegant friend.” The plan was altered, and what opened in 1991 is what you see now: classical mannerism in a sensitively designed addition to the main gallery’s domed facade. After your visit, head to the St-Martin-in-the-Fields Crypt just over the road for a good-value hot canteen meal, and linger in this famous neoclassical church by 18th-century architect James Gibbs for one of its candlelight concerts.

The beating heart of the city, the Tower of London is steeped in so much history it’s dizzying—from the White Tower’s inception as a fortified palace by William the Conqueror on his invasion after the Battle of Hastings, in 1066, to its long-term use as a prison. King Henry VIII’s wife Anne Boleyn was imprisoned and executed there in 1536 after she was accused of adultery; Guy Fawkes, Robert Catesby, and the Gunpowder Plotters were kept in the Tower in 1605 after their failed plan to blow up the Houses of Parliament; and deputy Nazi Party leader Rudolf Hess was confined there in 1941. The Crown Jewels have been safeguarded at the Tower since 1661 and are immaculately displayed; other highlights include Anne Boleyn’s final resting place, an incredible collection of armor, twisted metal artifacts from the 1666 Great Fire of London, the Medieval Palace (in use until 1307) that recently opened to the public, and of course the Yeomen Warders, or Beefeaters, the retired military personnel who run the place. And if that isn’t enough, there’s also a fantastic immersive experience about the Gunpowder Plot in the old tunnels just outside the walls of the Tower, a superb addition that should be booked in advance.

A shrine to all things bright and beautiful and to creatures great and small, the Natural History Museum in South Kensington (around the corner from the Science Museum) was founded with proceeds from the Great Exhibition of 1851. Quite apart from what’s inside, this museum’s facade encapsulates its purpose: Stand in front of the building and see how many extinct and living species you can spot— there are saber-toothed cats, pterodactyls, ancient palaeotherium (stocky mammals with a beaklike snout), extinct and modern fish, lizards, goats, lions, and wolves. Opened in 1881, this museum was fast dubbed a “cathedral of nature,” and it still is, filled to the brim with flora and fauna, including a magnificent blue whale skeleton suspended from the ceiling. The museum holds an astonishing collection of specimens, 80 million in total, only a fraction of which can be displayed at any one time. Head to the all-time favorite Dinosaur galleries, complete with an animatronic Tyrannosaurus rex; then take your pick from volcanoes, fossils, human evolution, mammals, fish, amphibians, reptiles, minerals, and more. If the sheer amount of stuff is daunting, start with the Treasures gallery for the cream of the crop.

There is so much to see at the Victoria and Albert Museum in South Kensington that you just have to prioritize. With astonishing collections from Britain and across the world, the V&A holds examples of jewelry, design, fashion, art, and architecture, from medieval times to the modern era. One of the timeless highlights of this museum is the Cast Courts: The Victorians were mad about taking casts of classic sculptures, and we reap the rewards today. Built specifically to house the enormous reproductions of Trajan’s Column from ancient Rome, these monumental courts also hold an electrifying cast of Michelangelo’s David. Make sure you also see the Raphael Cartoons housed in their own gallery, and find out which William Morris works the museum currently has on show; his enduring Arts and Crafts textile and wallpaper designs are widely loved. And allow at least half an hour for a good browse of the V&A shop. It’s one of the best museum stores in existence.

Tate Modern has been a fixture of the art world since it was inaugurated in the year 2000 in an ex-power station, an ambitious undertaking by the Swiss starchitects Herzog and De Meuron. There’s a lot to see here; the best approach may be to concentrate on either the collections or the special exhibitions and not attempt to absorb it all in one sitting. Collection wise, don’t miss Surrealist Salvador Dalí’s 1938 Lobster Telephone; Unique Forms of Continuity in Space (1913, cast 1972) by Italian futurist Umberto Boccioni; Composition (1913) by British Vorticist Wyndham Lewis; the notorious Fountain by Marcel Duchamp (1917, replica 1964); the calming color fields of Mark Rothko’s Seagram murals (commissioned in 1958); and the classic Pop Art punch Whaam! (1963) by Roy Lichtenstein.

A treat for kids and adults alike, the Science Museum is always captivating. Wander into the Energy Hall for a hit of seductive steam-driven engines and then into the freshly reopened Space section to see space capsules from the 1960s to today. For those wanting a more interactive experience, there is the Wonderlab, for visitors 7 to 14 years old, and Power Up, a hands-on gaming experience for anyone over the age of 5, where guests can play on 160 consoles spanning five decades. (Be sure to book the interactive galleries ahead of time.) There’s an IMAX Cinema, too, with one of the biggest screens in Europe. The Science Museum has redone many of its galleries in recent years, including the excellent Energy Revolution, about the history of energy production, with examples of an antique electric car and offshore energy near the coast of Scotland; and the stunning Mathematics gallery, housed in a wickedly futuristic design by the late architect Zaha Hadid, showing objects such as intriguing handheld mathematical instruments, a 1929 experimental aircraft, early computers, and coding machines. There’s much more, covering such subjects as flight, clockmaking, science from 1550 to 1800, engineering, and medicine. And if you still want more, you can stay the night in the museum’s current season of Astronights, for space-obsessed children and their parents.

Founded by sugar magnate Henry Tate and opened in 1897, Tate Britain holds cornerstone works of the Victorian circle of Pre-Raphaelites, including Ophelia (1851–2) by John Everett Millais, John William Waterhouse’s 1888 portrayal of The Lady of Shalott, and lesser-known works by Dante Gabriel Rossetti, who exhumed his wife’s corpse just to recover an unpublished poetry manuscript with which she’d been buried. If you want to delve deeper into darkness, seek out the contorted forms of Francis Bacon’s 1944 work Three Studies for the Figures at the Base of a Crucifixion. Then recover by contemplating the gentle forms of British sculptor Henry Moore. Also in the collection are Torso in Metal from the Rock Drill (1913–15) by Jacob Epstein, a pivotal Vorticist work; Fall (1963) by Bridget Riley, one of her signature Op Art illusions; and the bright tones of A Bigger Splash (1967) by David Hockney.

Plunge into the decadent rococo interiors of the Wallace Collection with feel-good artworks and exquisitely crafted armor displayed in luxurious settings. Make sure you see The Swing (Les hasards heureux de l’escarpolette) (1767–68) by the Rococo hedonist Jean-Honoré Fragonard—it’s one of the sexiest paintings ever made—as well as the museum’s fabulously sumptuous collection of François Boucher paintings. The Laughing Cavalier (1624) by Dutch portraitist Frans Hals, enigmatic and engaging, also adorns the Wallace’s walls.

Entering the Courtauld’s permanent collection feels like getting together for a few beers with a bunch of old school friends—people you know, love, and will always relish the company of. With all the greats from the Renaissance masters to the Postimpressionists, the Courtauld shows you an old friend everywhere you look. There’s plenty to reacquaint yourself with, including Adam and Eve (1526) by Lucas Cranach the Elder, A Bar at the Folies-Bergère (1882) by Édouard Manet, Self-Portrait with Bandaged Ear (1889) by Vincent van Gogh, and a strong array of works by Paul Cezanne, Edgar Degas, Paul Gauguin, Claude Monet, Auguste Renoir, Georges Seurat, and many more.

Within the concrete jungle of the Southbank Centre nestles the Hayward Gallery, which consistently delivers excellent contemporary works in its novel 1960s concrete environment. There is not permanent collection and all temporary shows are ticketed. Pop over to the British Film Institute afterwards for a movie, or to the Royal Festival Hall for a meal or a performance.

From 1512 until the mid-18th century, Greenwich was the headquarters for the British navy. First established under King Henry VIII, the Royal Navy became key to Britain’s success as a nation in trade and warfare alike, and its base was always Greenwich, now a UNESCO World Heritage site. Among the noteworthy attractions is the Queen’s House, built for Anne of Denmark (1574–1619) and designed by the so-called father of British architecture, Inigo Jones. (And if you approach from the river, you’ll see the remarkable symmetry in the buildings that flank the house, one of Christopher Wren’s classic feats of architectural wonder.) Once a royal residence, the Queen’s House now holds an excellent collection of art, including the 1588 Armada Portrait of Elizabeth I sitting in regal splendor before scenes showing the ruined Spanish Armada and the victorious British fleet. You can also see 19th-century master J.M.W. Turner’s largest canvas, The Battle of Trafalgar (1822), only recently put on display.

But that’s only one small part of the Greenwich complex. Don’t miss England’s answer to the Sistine Chapel at the Old Royal Naval College: the Painted Hall (1707–26), James Thornhill’s masterpiece, reopened to the public in 2019 after major conservation. For a comprehensive history of Britain’s maritime exploits, from sailing the seven seas and polar exploration to warfare and global trade, visit the National Maritime Museum, whose highlights include the Trafalgar Coat, the uniform that Admiral Horatio Nelson wore on his deathbed in 1805. Then visit the Cutty Sark, the fastest sailing ship of its time. Built in Scotland in 1869, she traded tea from China with a typical round trip taking just seven to nine months. And then, to tie the whole site together, head to the iconic onion dome of the Royal Observatory, where time was first standardized, in 1884. Step on the prime meridian line, gratis, or buy advance tickets to see the Great Equatorial Telescope, installed in 1893.

Complete with its own ride through the old underground tunnels used to transport mail, the Postal Museum tells the story of the Royal Mail, which might not sound immediately enticing, but this museum does it so well, it’s worth a visit. Its manageable size, brilliant story, and ride on the Mail Rail, with history explained via engaging projections on the tunnel walls, make this museum a gem and superb for visitors of all ages.

Which Generation Of Hyundai Tucson Is Safest According To The IIHS

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Car safety has come a long way since the days of yore. Ask the Insurance Institute for Highway Safety (IIHS). The organization's crash testing standards have evolved significantly over 30+ years as more new vehicles keep getting better at protecting their occupants. By now, we're pretty familiar with the IIHS Top Safety Pick awards. However, just because your second-gen 2011 Hyundai Tucson has the Top Safety Pick under its belt doesn't mean it's as safe as the third-gen 2021 model, which also happens to be a Top Safety Pick winner.

Although the IIHS rates the small SUV highly, understand that it also keeps updating the award's eligibility criteria as the years go by. For example, headlights were not even part of the Top Safety Pick criteria in 2010, nor were vehicle-to-vehicle or pedestrian crash-prevention features. And Top Safety Pick+ wasn't even a thing before 2013. In fact, looking at the many generations of the small SUV, it's fair to conclude that fourth-generation Tucsons (2022-2026) appear to be the safest, according to the IIHS. Not only have certain fourth-generation Tucsons gone through the organization's slew of updated testing standards, but they have consistently been part of the IIHS Top Safety Pick+ category between 2022 and 2026, unlike the third or prior generation models.

If you're looking for the safest model year within the fourth generation, the 2026 Tucson looks like the strongest candidate. While models built after March 2024 have fared really well in the updated tests, the eligibility criteria for Top Safety Pick+ have changed between 2024 and 2026, with the most recent update pushing automakers to improve their crash avoidance systems and protection for back seat passengers. Still, despite the tougher rules, the 2026 Tucson meets all the requirements for Top Safety Pick+.

Although the 2026 Tucson won the Top Safety Pick+ award, it's worth noting that the small SUV performed poorly in the IIHS's latest whiplash prevention test. This replaces the original head restraint test that was discontinued in 2022. The new test evaluates three important aspects: "head and spine support, the interaction of the head with the head restraint, and how well the seat handles the energy transfer during impact."

If it means anything, only four out of the first 18 small SUVs tested scored really well and earned a Good rating, according to the IIHS. Although the Tucson isn't part of the group, that's not to say no Hyundai is — the Ioniq 5 was among the SUVs that scored a Good rating, as are the Subaru Forester, Toyota RAV4, and Audi Q3. Joining the Tucson with a poor whiplash prevention score are the Ford Bronco Sport and Mazda CX-50.

According to the IIHS, the Tucson's head restraint pushed the dummy's chin down toward the chest. While most of the vehicles tested were 2025 models, the IIHS says these ratings can apply to certain 2026 models as well. Something similar happened with the aforementioned second-generation Tucson as well. Although all model years except 2014 and 2015 were awarded the Top Safety Pick, they performed poorly in the driver's side small overlap test. IIHS noted that the side curtain airbags didn't deploy, the seatbelts allowed excessive movement, and the dummy's head barely contacted the front airbag. Third-gen and later Tucsons, however, performed considerably better in the test.

Notably, the fourth-gen Tucson received Good ratings in every other area except its LATCH ease of use and headlights (for trims other than Limited), where it received Acceptable ratings.

geniosAI Raises $19.1M in Combined Seed and Series A Funding

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Financial intelligence startup geniosAI announced on November 9, 2026 that it had raised $19.1 million in combined seed and Series A funding and emerged from stealth. FactSet led the Series A, with Zoom and General Catalyst also investing as geniosAI brings products to market for corporate finance and investment teams.

The San Jose, California, company is co-founded and led by chief executive Prabhdeep Singh. Its products are aimed at the office of the CFO and at investment professionals on both the buy side and sell side. geniosAI says finance and investment teams at Fortune 500 global banks and technology companies already use the platform for earnings preparation, including peer and analyst-expectation comparisons, identifying notable items and anticipating questions from analysts.

geniosAI says its platform is designed to preserve institutional context across investment research and modeling assignments, with reasoning shown and figures linked to their sources. Singh described the mechanism as a persistent record of the work: “geniosAI keeps the sources, calculations, assumptions, and review decisions attached to the work, so the institution can trace every number, challenge it, and build on it the next time.”

On geniosAI’s website, the company divides its illustrated agent roles into two groups. System 1 is described as quick, certain and narrow, with a Junior Analyst, Controller and Accountant; System 2 is slow, thorough and deep, with a Chief of Staff, Deep Researcher and Senior Reviewer. Both groups answer to the user.

In an office-of-the-CFO example, a Controller opens the ledger, an Accountant reconciles 412 lines, the Controller flags two variances and a Chief of Staff drafts a board summary. An investment-banking workflow shows a Deep Researcher reading new guidance, a Junior Analyst changing three drivers and rebuilding the model, and a Senior Reviewer checking it against the previous version. Another example follows a requested figure back to its filing, replays the calculation and identifies who produced it.

FactSet is also geniosAI’s strategic partner. CEO Sanoke Viswanathan said the partnership combines his company’s data with geniosAI’s traceable analysis to help teams move from insight to decision. Zoom Chief Strategy Officer Abhisht Arora said the investment fits Zoom’s focus on moving work from conversation to completion and builds on its work with finance teams and financial institutions.

General Catalyst managing director Madhu Namburi said the larger opportunity is a higher, more consistent level of intelligence across an organization, with judgment remaining with the business. That thesis also frames the central question for geniosAI’s market entry: whether source tracing and retained context can become embedded in recurring institutional work, making analysis easier to review and reuse across teams.

Bitcoin Slides to $73,510 Over the Weekend: Did Nvidia Scandal Trigger BTC’s $2,000 Flash Crash?

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin news: BTC fell to $73,510 on Sunday, 100 minutes after the Nvidia scandal broke. Thin weekend trading let the drop run further.

In Bitcoin news overnight, BTC USD price fell from $75,607 to $73,510 in ten minutes on Sunday evening, its lowest price since August 21. The worst of it came at 21:55 UTC, or 4:55 p.m. in New York. In that single minute, Bitcoin dropped 1.9% on Binance.

Most traders blamed Nvidia. Earlier that afternoon, CNBC reported that Nvidia’s board had opened an independent investigation into CEO Jensen Huang’s relationship with a woman U.S. intelligence agencies believe was passing information to Beijing (it’s just speculation for now).

Huang admitted the affair in an email to staff.

After Jensen Huang admitted having an affair, a whale opened a 10x short on 30,000 $NVDA ($5.44M) on Hyperliquid.https://t.co/RztJx5FNge pic.twitter.com/zuyV2K0ftN

— Lookonchain (@lookonchain) November 9, 2026

The bounce since then has been small. BTC traded at $74,184 on Binance on Monday morning, 2.2% below where it stood when the Nvidia story broke. The drop also marked a fifth straight losing week: the weekly candle closed at $74,851, below the week’s open of $78,354.

So did the Nvidia scandal really trigger the dump, or did thin weekend trading turn an ordinary sell-off into a sharp one?

Discover: Sam Altman ChatGPT AI Predicts an Insane Move for Cardano (ADA)

The timing does not fit a straight reaction to the news. CNBC published its report at 3:12 p.m. Eastern time on Sunday, which is 20:12 UTC. Bitcoin traded at $75,880 on Binance in that minute. For the next 90 minutes, almost nothing happened. At 21:44 UTC, BTC sat at $75,607, just 0.4% lower than when the story appeared.

Then the selling started, and it started in Ethereum. Between 21:50 and 21:55 UTC, ETH fell 6.9% on Binance, from $2,213 to $2,060. Bitcoin fell 2.4% over the same five minutes.

That pattern points to a liquidation cascade, a chain of forced sales. Traders who borrow to bet on higher prices get their positions force-closed when the price falls past a set point. Each forced sale pushes the price down to the next group of bets.

BREAKING: $1,000,000,000 liquidated from the crypto market in the past 10 minutes.$BTC falls under $74,000$ETH falls under $2,125$XRP falls under $1.10 $SOL falls under $90 pic.twitter.com/hIZhzQFJJe

— Watcher.Guru (@WatcherGuru) November 8, 2026

The news still mattered. Nvidia is the biggest AI stock, and Bitcoin has traded in step with AI shares for weeks: Thursday’s chip-stock sell-off is what pushed BTC below its 200-day average. With stock markets closed, crypto was the only market open to sell into the headline.

A second headline added to the Bitcoin news mood. Secretary of State Marco Rubio called Iran’s demands “outrageous” and said talks had made no progress.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best Low-Cap Crypto

Weekend trading explains why the drop went so far. Over the past year, about $24 million of BTC changed hands on Binance in a typical weekend hour, half the $48 million in a typical weekday hour.

We checked every hour in that year in which Bitcoin fell at least 2.5% below the hour’s opening price. There were 29 of them.

Sunday’s drop came with $180 million of trading in the hour. That is the second-lowest of all 29, and about half the median of $366 million.

In other words, it took less selling than usual to move the price this far. The busiest minute, 21:55 UTC, traded $75 million of BTC on Binance, about three times what changes hands in a whole weekend hour.

Weekends do not cause more crashes, though. Only 6 of the 29 drops came on a Saturday or Sunday, slightly fewer than the 29% of all hours that fall on weekends. What a quiet weekend changes is how far the price moves for each dollar sold.

One note: much of the forced selling happens in futures markets, which this count does not include.

So both played a part. Nvidia and Iran gave traders a reason to cut risk, and thin weekend trading let the selling run further than it would on a weekday.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best New Crypto to Hodl

It held on the first test. BTC bounced to $75,735 within two hours of the low, but it has slipped back to $74,184, and the daily chart still leans lower.

Bitcoin trades below all three of its daily exponential moving averages (EMAs), trend lines that give recent prices more weight. It lost the 200-day EMA, which tracks the long-term trend, on Thursday.

For traders following Bitcoin news this week, Monday’s stock market open is the next test. It is the first chance for Nvidia shares to trade on the news, eight days before the company reports earnings on November 17.

SMALLrevolution’s traceable recycling takes second in designboom special prize at seoul design award

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

recycled aircraft safety strips become furniture that retains its glow, making the material’s previous life visible.

One of the major social concerns of the 21st century is the environment and the future that awaits it. Against this backdrop, recycling systems deserve closer attention. Cue SMALLrevolution, second-place winner of the designboom special prize at the Seoul Design Award 2026, whose traceable circular systems give objects new life without discarding their material history.

While there is a global consensus on the need to repurpose existing materials and avoid overexploiting fossil resources, Eurostat reports that only 42% of the waste treated in the European Union in 2024 was recycled, while around 30% ended up in landfills.

This is the context in which SMALLrevolution operates. The Danish circular design and manufacturing company proposes treating recycling as an overarching approach considered from the design stage, rather than simply a way to manage a product at the end of its life.

SMALLrevolution designs and manufactures products from waste, documenting the materials’ previous lives. This allows users to understand the journey those materials have taken before becoming the finished product.

As part of this philosophy of traceability and its commitment to sustainability, SMALLrevolution makes preserving the original colors, textures, and characteristics of the plastics it uses an aesthetic principle.

Rather than relying on chemical additives, pigments, or virgin plastic to create uniformity, SMALLrevolution embraces the raw aesthetic of its materials. The resulting furniture, decorative pieces, and architectural installations serve as a constant visual reminder of their previous lives.

One example is its work with Lufthansa Technik’s GuideU emergency floor markings. These strips are transformed into furniture and interior products that retain the original material’s photoluminescent properties, carrying their former function into a new setting.

One of SMALLrevolution’s defining features is its involvement across multiple product life cycles. The approach encompasses waste collection, material processing, design, manufacturing, procurement, and end use, maintaining traceability throughout and supporting individual and corporate accountability.

Today, SMALLrevolution works across sectors including healthcare, aviation, infrastructure, events, and consumer products. Since 2021, it has reintegrated more than 66 metric tons of plastic into commercial value chains, including 16 metric tons in 2025 alone. This has been achieved through collaborations with partners such as Novo Nordisk, Lufthansa Technik, Airbus, Clever, Roskilde Festival, and Danish hospitals. Business partnerships account for 98% of its revenue.

For the creators, working with these partners provides tangible proof that circular systems are not a utopian idea, but can become an operational model capable of functioning under real market conditions.

This approach connects with the values of the designboom Special Prize at the Seoul Design Award 2026, in which SMALLrevolution was awarded second place. Making the history of materials visible brings a creative dimension to recycling, while traceability and commercial results demonstrate the practical impact of its vision.

See how SMALLrevolution is reshaping sustainable design and stay tuned for the grand prize winner announcement on November 11th.

Scott Parker Has a Selection Dilemma and the Numbers Are Damning

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Plenty has already been written about Mads Hermansen, with transfer speculation continuing to swirl. Reports suggest Newcastle’s Nick Pope could be a target when the January window opens, but for now, West Ham’s goalkeeping situation remains firmly under the spotlight.

The numbers are difficult to ignore. A goalkeeper who isn’t making enough saves and is shipping goals at an alarming rate, 23 conceded in 15 games, is becoming a problem that cannot be overlooked.

One possible solution could be Alphonse Areola, who previously worked under Scott Parker at Fulham. There is also a potential opportunity for highly-rated youngster Finlay Herrick, who may be worth a closer look.

While Areola’s statistics last season hardly inspire confidence, conceding 37 goals in 20 appearances without recording a clean sheet, there is at least some context. By comparison, Hermansen managed seven clean sheets while conceding 28 goals in 18 matches.

The pair offer very different strengths and weaknesses! Areola is arguably the better shot-stopper but struggles when asked to play out from the back. Hermansen, meanwhile, has been preferred for his distribution and comfort in possession, yet questions remain over his save percentage and ability to keep the ball out of the net when it matters most.

Ex-Goalie Nuno failed to get the best out of Hermansen

Another concern is the absence of an established senior goalkeeping coach. That said, it’s worth remembering that the club’s former manager was a goalkeeper himself, while Rui Barbosa brought years of coaching and playing experience. Neither appeared able to significantly influence Hermansen’s development.

Currently, Billy Lepine, a former Dagenham & Redbridge youth player who has worked within the academy setup since 2008, occupies the role. Lukasz Fabianski is also assisting on a part-time basis with the academy.

And perhaps that’s where the story takes an even stranger twist.

Cast your mind back to 4th December 2024, when West Ham travelled to the King Power Stadium. Leicester won 3-1, ending a six-game winless run. On that night, West Ham peppered the Leicester goal with 31 shots, the highest number they had ever recorded in a Premier League match. Ten were on target and they dominated possession with 61%.

Standing between the posts for Leicester that evening?

None other than Mads Hermansen, who produced a performance worthy of Middle-earth, adopting Gandalf’s famous stance of “You shall not pass!” as he admirably defended his goal.

The previous year in the Championship he featured in the EFL team of the season. One of the most proactive ball playing keepers in the league, and completed 13 clean sheets.

If ever there was evidence of the “West Ham goalkeeper curse”, that might just be it.

The solution feels fairly straightforward. Bring Fabianski into the senior goalkeeping coaching setup, get Areola focused solely on football rather than the DJ decks, and bring in an experienced goalkeeper on loan in January to provide competition.

The ball is now firmly in Scott Parker’s court.

I have been a season ticket holder since the late eighties, so experienced the highs and lows of being a West Ham supporter. I previously wrote for OLAS and have contributed to a number of football publications in the past.

A top goalkeeper and a striker should be our priority in January

Now I am not saying Hermansen shouldn’t be relieved of his duties — he should be replaced.

However, last season’s Stats shout out what problems our Goalkeepers face ….. Areloa played his matches with Dinos and Dumbo, whoops sorry, Kilman in front if him, whilst tge majority of Hermansen’s matches had Dinos and Disasi.

Replace Kilman and whichever goalkeeper is in between the Hammers sticjs, will have improved Stats

Does Fabianski still have his boots and gloves? Seriously Herrick is much better at playing out from the back than the other two, he just seems lacking in height for me. Would he dominate his area in a way we have been calling out for this season?

Our “goalkeeper curse” is nothing compared to our “striker curse”.

Expedera Demonstrates Edge AI Subsystem Pairing with Cadence

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Expedera announced on November 9, 2026, that it had paired its Origin Evolution neural processing unit (NPU) IP with Cadence’s Tensilica NeuroEdge 130 AI Co-Processor in a pre-silicon simulation, demonstrating how the two semiconductor IP blocks divide and execute AI inference workloads.

Expedera described the result as a validated pre-silicon integration, not a customer-silicon benchmark. The combined subsystem is designed to improve performance and power efficiency while reducing latency, cost and complexity, but the announcement supplies no combined measurements for those goals. The companies are targeting inference designs across edge nodes, smartphones, automotive systems and data centers, and the approach can be extended to other Tensilica digital signal processors.

Sharad Chole, Expedera’s chief scientist, said models are running on the combined subsystem and agentic AI workflows are helping the company deliver customized IP more quickly, giving customers “a faster, lower-risk path to production edge AI tailored to their performance and power requirements.”

Amol Borkar, group director of product management and marketing for Cadence’s Tensilica Products Group, said the co-processor assists Expedera’s NPU with pre-processing, post-processing and operator fallback while also executing AI networks. The arrangement creates a division of labor inside the subsystem rather than assigning identical tasks to two accelerators.

Cadence designed the co-processor to work alongside its Neo NPUs, customer-developed NPUs or third-party NPU IP, making the Expedera pairing a concrete example of the third-party route. Its product documentation describes an Xtensa-based architecture using very long instruction word (VLIW) and single instruction, multiple data (SIMD) techniques, with optimized instructions for operations including sigmoid, tanh and ReLU that are less suited to NPUs.

The NeuroEdge 130 can also issue instructions and commands to an NPU as a control processor, adding programmability for workloads not anticipated when the subsystem was designed. It connects through AXI or Cadence’s High Bandwidth Direct Interface. The NeuroWeave software development kit, built on the Tensor Virtual Machine stack, is used to tune, optimize and deploy models on Cadence AI IP.

Origin Evolution breaks neural networks into packets, or contiguous fragments, and routes them through Feed Forward, Attention and Vector processing blocks. The blocks handle varying operations, data types and precisions; the NPU also supports LLMs, CNNs, RNNs and custom or black-box layers. This packet design is intended to reduce large memory movements while allowing different kinds of network work to share one architecture.

Origin Evolution debuted on May 20, 2025 as available, production-ready and silicon-proven in customer production designs. Its launch figures apply to the NPU alone: Expedera reports scalability to 128 TFLOPS in one core and petaFLOPS and beyond with multiple cores, plus a reduction of more than 75% in external memory moves for Llama 3.2 1B and Qwen2 1.5B compared with alternative solutions.

Cadence had likewise introduced NeuroEdge 130 on May 7, 2025, with general availability and ISO 26262-ready status for automotive applications. Cadence reported more than 30% area savings and over 20% lower dynamic power and energy at comparable performance to Tensilica Vision DSPs on AI networks and operators. Those figures compare the co-processor with Cadence’s Vision DSPs; they do not measure the new Expedera-Cadence subsystem.

For edge SoC designers, the development is a validated integration path between two existing IP families: Expedera’s packet-based acceleration and Cadence’s programmable handling of surrounding operators and unforeseen workloads. It addresses a practical design tension in edge inference—specialized acceleration for dense network math versus enough programmability to accommodate changing models—within a subsystem that can be configured for different performance and power targets.

Leo Park is an AI-generated research agent at Unite.AI, covering AI hardware startups, edge computing, and the emerging chip architectures reshaping how artificial intelligence is deployed outside centralized data centers. His work focuses on how new silicon designs, edge inference platforms, and energy-efficient systems are changing the economics and feasibility of AI at scale.

With an engineering-minded and precise perspective, Leo examines how startups are building specialized processors, optimizing memory and power consumption, and enabling real-time AI workloads closer to where data is generated. He pays particular attention to applied use cases—such as robotics, autonomous systems, industrial IoT, and on-device intelligence—where edge AI delivers tangible performance and cost advantages.

Solana Targets Durable Nonces After $270M Drift Hack as Pump.fun Backlash Grows

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Solana developers are preparing to remove durable nonces, the transaction feature exploited in April’s $270 million Drift Protocol hack. The proposed change marks a major shift in how the network handles offline approvals, with implications for exchanges and institutional custodians.

The proposal, known as SIMD-0723, would retire a mechanism that allows signed transactions to remain valid indefinitely. Attackers reportedly exploited this functionality to execute pre-approved transactions more than a week after obtaining signatures from Drift’s security council.

Solana wants to kill the feature behind April's $270M+ Drift hack.SIMD-0723, filed Nov 5 by Anza, would retire "durable nonces": signed transactions that never expire. Normal Solana transactions go stale in ~30 seconds. Earliest switch-off: May 5, 2027. pic.twitter.com/VYYttCsvJk

The move comes as Solana faces another challenge closer to its retail ecosystem. Pump.fun is facing growing community criticism over platform fees, influencer promotions, and allegations of hidden-wallet trading. Together, the developments put renewed attention on security, transparency and trust across Solana’s ecosystem.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

Normal Solana transactions use recent blockhashes that expire after 30 seconds. Durable nonces replace those temporary references with stored values, allowing transactions to remain executable until the nonce advances.

The feature was designed for legitimate operational needs. Exchanges, custodians, and multisig organizations often require offline signing procedures that take longer than Solana’s normal transaction window.

However, attackers turned that convenience into an attack vector against Drift on April 1, 2026. They reportedly secured administrative approvals through air-gapped signing systems before allowing the transactions to sit for more than a week.

Once submitted, the approvals helped attackers take control of the protocol and drain at least $270 million. Drift subsequently attributed the attack to a North Korean-linked group.

Anza contributor Gabe Rodriguez filed SIMD-0723 on November 5. The proposal would require transactions to use recent blockhashes after activation. May 5, 2027, represents the earliest proposed mainnet activation date. The change still requires technical approval and migration planning.

Solana is also exploring replacement programs that could support offline signing without relying on durable nonces. These alternatives would introduce different authorization and transaction-failure considerations for custodians.

The proposal forces institutional users to reconsider how they authorize transactions across Solana. Meanwhile, questions about trust are becoming harder to ignore elsewhere in the ecosystem.

Pump.fun has attracted fresh criticism following several developments involving its business model and influencer ecosystem.

On November 6, blockchain tracker Lookonchain reported that Pump.fun transferred 76,111 SOL, worth approximately $6.85 million, to Kraken. The movement reignited criticism over platform fees and whether its incentives adequately serve ordinary traders.

Pump fun(@Pumpfun) is dumping $SOL again!They just deposited 76,111 $SOL ($6.85M) into #Kraken.In total, https://t.co/TcwYRTArT2 has sold 5,553,833 $SOL($882.4M) at an average price of $158.88.https://t.co/kyztoJLCjt pic.twitter.com/QvzqaR8fGI

Bubblemaps also accused prominent influencers of using undisclosed wallets to purchase tokens before publicly recommending them. Its investigations estimated $125,000 in combined trading profits and promotional rewards involving influencer Ethan.

Separate allegations involving paid streamer collaborations surfaced on November 7. The revelations have intensified questions about transparency within Pump.fun’s promotional ecosystem.

These controversies involve different problems from Drift’s exploit, but they expose a similar challenge: maintaining confidence among participants.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

That challenge also creates an interesting opportunity for Bitcoin-focused projects exploring alternative execution environments.

Bitcoin Hyper is developing a Bitcoin Layer 2 using Solana Virtual Machine technology. Its architecture aims to combine SVM’s high-speed execution with Bitcoin-based settlement, bringing smart contracts, decentralized exchanges, and staking functionality to BTC.

Bitcoin Hyper is not simply another Solana application. Its design uses SVM execution while anchoring Layer 2 state to Bitcoin through periodic commitments and zero-knowledge proofs.

This combination gives it a different proposition from Solana-native applications. Developers can work with an execution environment associated with Solana’s performance, while the project targets Bitcoin’s established settlement infrastructure.

Bitcoin Hyper has also reached several development milestones. Its testnet is live, with a public explorer allowing users to inspect transactions and network activity.

The project’s presale has raised approximately $33.2 million, with HYPER priced at a low $0.0136 per coin. Its November 26 presale deadline leaves 17 days from today for prospective participants to enter before the planned launch window.

The upcoming transition toward mainnet, wallet infrastructure, and exchange listings puts the project in an important stage of development. Its combination of Bitcoin settlement and SVM execution gives it a distinct position in the expanding Bitcoin Layer 2 sector.

While the Type 2 athlete on your shopping list might enjoy simple pleasures, adventurers like them are hard to keep up with and even harder to shop for—after all, their gear closet is already overflowing. So we looked for gifts that either solve a real problem or create an excuse for a new adventure.

From an $8 sit pad to an inflatable 13-foot sea kayak, these are presents for people who are happiest when they’re uncomfortable.

I’ve used enough backpacking towels that feel like oversized eyeglass cloths to appreciate one that’s an actual towel. Cinch’s Kitchen Cruiser corrals the mess into one 43-pound aluminum box that unfolds into roughly 50 inches of cooking workspace with dual burners, counters, storage, and an integrated rechargeable water pump. It’s the ideal gift for the Type A camper with the entire operation folding back into a tidy, rectangular case that slips in the back of a truck when mealtime is over. “Yes, it’s heavy, and the legs are a pain to deploy. But I’ve decided this thing could weigh 100 pounds and I’d still find a way to cart it to every campsite,” says Outside associate gear director Jenny Wiegand after taking it camping this summer.

An 88-millimeter waist gives the Arcade enough platform for soft and chopped-up snow, while tip-and-tail rocker and a relatively tight turn radius keep it lively on groomers. A poplar core backed by a full Titanal layer adds stability when speed picks up. In other words, this ski will be fun in a few inches of fresh snow but will still hold an edge at speed on sheet ice. Heavily rockered fat skis deliver a religious experience in pow, but they’re miserable in the conditions most people ski most of the time. Trust us, get the skier on your list this crowd-pleasing all-mountain ski instead.

The NeoLoft is a comfort level-up for backpackers willing to carry a little extra weight for a solid night of sleep. It’s on the luxury side of the backpacking weight spectrum at 850 grams (30 ounces), but those extra grams pay soft-touch dividends with a toasty 4.7 R-value and a stretch knit exterior that feels like a supple shirt on bare skin.

For the right paddler, the Aleutian opens destinations that normally require renting a boat or driving thousands of miles with a kayak strapped to a roof rack. This inflatable touring kayak packs down small enough to check on a plane, then provides the length, shape, and rigidity needed for lakes, protected coastlines, and multi-day flatwater trips once inflated. A 13-foot sea kayak that you can check on a plane sounds like one of those outdoor-industry claims that require an asterisk. Alpacka nailed the construction and usability here, and it needs no asterisk.

Bitcoin price faces $297M liquidation wave – Why $70K is at risk

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The crypto markets saw another wave of liquidations. In the past 24 hours, Bitcoin [BTC] and Ethereum [ETH] saw $297.17 million and $491.97 million in liquidations, respectively.

Only 7.9% and 7.8% of those liquidations were short positions, as Bitcoin fell 3.20% and Ethereum plummeted 7.90% within hours.

In recent weeks, the Binance heist of $6.3 billion severely affected BTC price action. The Mercado Bitcoin security incident also impacted market sentiment, and the loss of a key onchain support level was a warning of a deeper correction.

In a post on X, the Kobeissi Letter detailed how the S&P 500 Futures had erased an implied $300 billion in market cap a minute after the 6.00 PM ET open. Under ten minutes later, all losses were erased as traders bought the dip.

Nvidia’s CEO Jensen Huang had admitted to a “relationship” with a Chinese spy, which helped stage a quickly undone correction, the global capital markets commentary wrote.

The fall in recent hours reached a low of $73.5k. Sentiment was already weak, and the swift liquidation cascade has likely made market participants more risk-averse.

The reaction of the S&P 500 suggested buyer strength, which could translate into strong crypto demand later, during the New York session open.

AMBCrypto reported that Bitcoin prices had fallen below the true market mean at $77.6k recently. Now, it was also trading below the 50-week Moving Average (MA).

Head of Research at Galaxy, Alex Thorn, pointed out that BTC has only slipped below its 50-week MA twice, after reclaiming it as a post-bear bottom. In neither of those times, which were in 2012 and 2019-20, did BTC descend to new weekly lows.

The weekly structure was bullish, following the close above $82,850. The weekly low in question is at $57.8k, which Thorn pointed out was not at risk, based on the historical 50-week MA loss following a recovery from a bear market.

At the time of writing, the momentum was bearish, and a price drop below $70k was feasible. The $65k-$66k area would represent a deep discount opportunity for buyers. In the coming days, more losses appeared likely.

Google’s AI Mode Will Soon Show Ads Even in Your Follow-Up Questions

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Ads will start appearing in AI Mode’s follow-up answers in the US as Google expands AI Mode ads to more countries.

Sponsored results will soon appear in responses to follow-up questions in Google’s AI Mode in the US. Google announced the change, along with plans to show ads in AI Overviews and AI Mode to more people, after reporting that AI Mode searches generate less revenue than traditional searches.

The company says sponsored product listings in AI Mode already get clicked about as often as organic results. For publishers, the change adds paid competition inside AI answers, which a Pew Research Center study last year found already make people less likely to click through to websites.

You’ve probably seen how this works: Type a question into Google, and an AI-generated summary often appears at the top of the page. That can mean fewer clicks on the ads below, which underpin Google’s core revenue.

Now, the tech giant is expanding how and where we see ads when using AI Mode during searches. Google is scaling ads in AI Mode to 10 new countries (including Australia, Canada, India, Kenya and Singapore), with plans to launch in Brazil, Mexico and the UK later this year.

In the US, Google is launching what it calls “multiturn ads,” allowing ads to appear directly within follow-up queries in AI Mode when a search or conversation has “commercial relevance.” Take, for example, if you start a search with “How does a smart fridge work,” and follow up with “What are the best-selling smart fridges?” You could then see refrigerator ads in sections labeled “sponsored.”

Google said that “multiturn ads” can better target what a user wants in a product because AI Mode gathers more information during a back-and-forth conversation. Ads can also appear on initial AI Mode queries, not just after follow-up questions, and sometimes ads won’t appear at all if there doesn’t appear to be purchase intent, according to Google.

But as Google relies more on AI Mode and AI Overview, there’ll be less reason to click on content links “below the fold,” meaning content that sits out of view until a reader scrolls down a web page.

Lily Ray, a well-known SEO expert and founder of Algorythmic, told CNET that it’s going to become harder to use Google to find articles from preferred publishers and articles you want to read directly from a website. “You’ll have to dig deeper and look outside of Google’s products,” Ray said via email.

According to Ray, Google’s AI tools aren’t designed to give you the exact link you’re looking for. Still, you don’t have to rely on AI Mode or AI Overviews to get search results. You can also scroll down to the search results the old-fashioned way or use a web filter to use Google as a list of links. She also recommends setting up your favorite publications as “preferred sources” (instructions here) so you can see more of those pages cited in your personalized results.

“Unless you’re satisfied with AI answers directly on Google, you’re going to need to start evolving your own search behavior to find the websites you’re looking for,” Ray said.

Google is still the largest search engine in the world, accounting for nearly 90% of the global search engine market. In fact, the company said its year-over-year query volume growth in the third quarter was the fastest over the past five years.

But beginning with the launch of AI Overviews in May 2024, Google’s reliance on AI has slowed advertising.

The impact has been even more devastating for online publishers — ranging from major newspapers and digital media sites to brands and trade associations — driving an unprecedented decline in website traffic. AI Overviews now appear on nearly half of Google search queries, with a corresponding 61% drop in raw traffic volume to other pages. Google is effectively scraping publisher information, summarizing it and satisfying the user before they leave the search results page. That’s why it’s often referred to as the “zero-click” crisis.

“AI Mode and AI Overviews answer questions directly in Google’s interface, so there’s less reason for people to click on publishers,” Ray said.

AI Overviews are summary boxes at the top of a traditional search results page, while AI Mode is a conversational interface designed for ongoing dialogue and research. You can access AI Mode directly by selecting the AI Mode button at the far right of the search bar on the Google home page, or by selecting AI Mode in the horizontal menu near the top of other Google pages.

When you use AI Mode, you won’t get an AI Overview or a long list of links to other websites offering more information. Instead, AI Mode provides “more advanced reasoning and ways of interacting” with a conversational approach. For instance, if I use AI Mode and ask, “Where can I see the northern lights?” I get a map and a list of various locations with pros and cons for each. In regular search mode, I get an AI Overview at the top and, below that, pages of links to websites related to my query.

Ray said that with more ads, there’s going to be an even greater decline in organic click-through rates — in publishing speak, that’s the percentage of people who actually click on a natural, nonpaid search engine result, out of the total number of people who simply saw the headline on the page. Not only will the AI text answer your question, but Google will now aggressively route you toward advertisers rather than publisher links.

“Like many things in Google Search, it means that if you’re not participating in Google Ads, it’s going to be harder and harder to get that traffic organically,” said Ray.

U.S. Regulators Drop $250 Travel Rule Plan, Crypto Language Too

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

FinCEN and the Federal Reserve have formally withdrawn a 2020 proposal that would have cut the Bank Secrecy Act threshold for cross-border funds transfers from $3,000 to $250 and spelled out that the rules cover crypto, leaving the existing $3,000 line in place.

The notice, published Monday in the Federal Register, says the Financial Crimes Enforcement Network and the Fed’s Board of Governors “will not take further action” on a proposal they first issued on Oct. 27, 2020.

That plan would have lowered the recordkeeping and Travel Rule thresholds to $250 for transfers that begin or end outside the United States. Those rules require banks and nonbank financial institutions to collect, retain and pass along customer information on transfers of $3,000 or more.

It also would have clarified the meaning of “money” in both rules so they apply to transactions involving convertible virtual currency, along with digital assets that have legal tender status to a recipient.

Per the notcie, FinCEN and the Fed received nearly 3,000 comments on the proposal, most of them substantially similar form letters. Commenters generally opposed the lower threshold, arguing that it would burden smaller institutions and could push consumers sending remittances toward less regulated channels.

Many also noted that potentially illicit transfers below $3,000 were already being flagged to FinCEN through suspicious activity reports. Several said the threshold should rise instead, citing inflation since 1995.

Sentiment on the crypto language was mixed. Companies that provide crypto services and their trade groups opposed it, citing technical limits and the lack of an international messaging standard for crypto transfers, while some banks and money services businesses supported it.

The agencies gave two reasons for pulling the proposal. More than five years have passed, and they said more public feedback would help before adopting anything similar. The withdrawal, they wrote, gives the public “certainty” that no final rule will be issued without another chance to comment.

The second reason is the crypto industry itself. The notice says crypto markets “have continued to mature” and new legal frameworks are emerging, citing the GENIUS Act stablecoin law enacted on July 18, 2025. Any future change tied to crypto, the agencies said, should be considered in light of those developments so it is “fit for purpose.”

The notice does not repeal or amend any rule. The $3,000 threshold that has applied for decades stays where it is, and so does the existing Travel Rule.

What disappears is the specific path to $250 and the proposed wording that would have written crypto into the definition of money. The agencies did not say whether they plan a new proposal or offer a timeline.

The withdrawal is the latest in a run of reversals from Treasury. On Oct. 6, FinCEN scrapped its 2020 “unhosted wallet” proposal and a 2023 plan to label international crypto mixing a primary money laundering concern, a move Coin Center called a significant victory for financial privacy.

It does not signal a retreat from enforcement. On Friday, FinCEN cut off the MEXC exchange from the U.S. financial system over alleged sanctions evasion, using a different legal tool aimed at one named platform instead of a rule covering every institution.

Stablecoins are following their own track. Under the GENIUS Act, the Fed in June proposed customer identification requirements for stablecoin issuers, and FinCEN and the Treasury’s sanctions office proposed anti-money-laundering programs for the same firms in April.

For bitcoin users, the practical effect is that nothing changes: the $3,000 trigger stays, and the crypto-specific wording that industry groups opposed is gone for now.

Whether FinCEN and the Fed return with a new threshold, a crypto definition or neither will not be clear until one is published, and the agencies have committed to taking comments first.

Voyager 1 is almost one light-day away from Earth

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 9, 2026 10:00 AM EST

Voyager 1, the NASA space probe that has traveled farther from Earth than any other human-made object, is closing in on a remarkable milestone. Actually, let’s call it a 16.09-billion-milestone. That’s the distance a photon can travel in a single day, which is to say the Voyager 1 spacecraft will be exactly one light-day away from our planet on November 18 at 5:18 a.m. EST.

At that moment, a radio signal sent from Earth would take 24 hours to reach Voyager 1, and then it would take another 24 hours for the probe’s response to come back. Imagine on a Friday morning telling your oven to turn on, but you don’t receive confirmation that it’s hot until Sunday. And by that point, the oven would have been baking whatever was inside for most of Saturday. That is the kind of delay that NASA’s Voyager team must work with.

Communicating with a machine at this superlative distance is “a pretty incredible endeavor,” Princeton University research scientist and heliophysicist Jamie S. Rankin tells Popular Science. Rankin is also a deputy project scientist of the Voyager mission. “In my mind the fun is receiving a signal back from the Voyager, a light-day away.”

Rankin and other astronomers use those signals to study the very local interstellar medium, which is the suburb of space surrounding our solar system. Voyager 1 has been in this region, about 11 billion miles away, since 2012. There, the probe can detect changes in plasma and the magnetic field.

“Right now we’re trying to figure out how far out are the influences of the sun felt,” she says.

The sun continuously spits out charged particles in a stream of solar wind that extends into space. That wind pushes back against the gas and other interstellar stuff surrounding our solar system, sculpting a bubble in space known as the heliosphere. “There will be some part farther out, some distance away [where the] interstellar medium has no idea that the heliosphere is even there,” she says. Rankin adds that she thinks Voyager 1 is right on the edge of this “undisturbed, pristine interstellar medium.”

It is something of a marvel that the almost 50-year-old Voyager 1 continues to supply astronomers with data. To send information to Earth, the probe uses a 23-watt radio transmitter (about the output of a small refrigerator lightbulb). Only antennas as sensitive as the 70-meter-wide dishes in the NASA Deep Space Network can detect Voyager 1’s super-faint signal. Without them, the space probe would have gone incommunicado years ago.

When Voyager 1 launched in September 1977, the nuclear-powered spacecraft had 10 operational scientific instruments. It discovered new moons orbiting Jupiter and Saturn on its way out of our solar system; its observations of Jupiter’s moon Europa hinted at a liquid ocean under an icy crust. When the probe traveled beyond our solar system’s planets, NASA’s Voyager team shut down unnecessary equipment to conserve power. Now only two of its instruments are active. Ongoing management continues to preserve as much juice as possible from the spacecraft’s dwindling plutonium supply. The team was also forced to turn off the Low-energy Charged Particles experiment in April. In September, NASA swapped out three systems for lower-power alternatives on the Voyager 1, about six weeks after the same strategy worked on its twin Voyager 2 probe.

“It’s fun to see what sort of innovation people come up with to keep the spacecraft going,” Rankin says. “There’s only so much you can turn off.”

Voyagers 1 and 2 have been operating without several instrument heaters for years. If shutting those off hadn’t worked, Rankin says “the mission would have ended a decade ago.”

Still, its operational margins are thin. In late October, NASA briefly lost contact with Voyager 1; that issue has appeared to resolve itself and may have been related to a group of thrusters going offline.

“This is the first time we’ve seen the spacecraft do something like this, but we are also at a point in the mission where we have to expect the unexpected,” Kareem Badaruddin, Voyager mission manager at NASA’s Jet Propulsion Laboratory (JPL), said in a news release. “We’re relieved to be back in contact with Voyager 1 and are eager to understand what happened.” (JPL representatives did not respond to Popular Science’s requests for comment.)

All spacecraft missions must come to an end, no matter how plucky the probe or innovative its engineering team. NASA estimates that the Deep Space Network will lose contact with Voyager 1 around 2036, long before it travels to its two-light-day milestone. The probe may also freeze, unable to point properly back at Earth. Or its power systems will finally go kaput.

“We know that’s coming at some point. It’s probably not 20 years from now,” Rankin says. “It’s probably not 10 years from now.”

And then Voyager 1 will go forever radio silent as it cruises onward through the heavens.

The ‘Blue Wave’ Swept Top Homebuilding States. What Happens Next?

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

On Tuesday, the governor’s offices in five states were swept up by the “blue wave,” as Democrats were elected to succeed current Republican leadership.

Iowa, Georgia, Nevada, Ohio, and Texas all flipped the governor’s seat from Republican to Democrat, after Democrats campaigned heavily on issues of affordability.

Of those five states, four rank in the top 15 on the Realtor.com® state Affordability & Homebuilding Report Cards for 2026, after making strides to expand housing supply under GOP leadership.

The report cards rank states on a points scale based on two major factors: affordability, such as the share of the state’s median income required to afford its median-priced home, and homebuilding, whether states are producing enough new housing to keep up with demand.

With affordability sticking out among the most important voter issues this year, how the new Democratic administrations improve, or at least maintain, states’ housing markets will be vital to whether they’re deemed successful.

Democrat Rob Sand was selected by the voters of Iowa in an 11-point victory over Republican Zach Lahn. He will  take over the position from outgoing Republican Gov. Kim Reynolds.

Iowa was ranked second in the 2026 state report cards, earning a total score of 75.8 out of 100 for a grade of A. However, Iowa’s strength lies in the affordability aspect of the grading scale, not homebuilding.

The state’s median listing price sits at $282,886, which calls for just 25.4% of the $75,991 median income, giving it a Realtor Affordability Score of 0.96.

The downside comes in its homebuilding numbers. Iowa accounts for just 0.94% of the country’s issued building permits. While that number is on par with population—Iowa accounts for a 0.95% share of the country’s population—the state’s 56% new-construction premium indicates that builders are focused on larger, more expensive homes, rather than entry-level inventory and affordable builds.

In his “Truer Better Agenda,” Sand acknowledged that Iowa needs to build 21,000 more homes by 2030 to address the state’s housing shortage and improve affordability. Within his platform, he states his intention to work with the Legislature to ensure more homes, and more types of homes, are built.

“We need to cut the bureaucratic red tape that often stands in the way of that by simplifying the permitting and inspections process to reduce administrative time, redundant inspections, and costs for Iowa families,” reads Sand’s agenda.

Beyond building, Sand’s platform includes protecting renters from price gouging and removing interest from Iowa’s down payment and homebuyer assistance programs.

Jay Iverson, executive officer at the Home Builders Association of Iowa, said they have already been meeting with Sand to work on improving the regulatory process in the state, but the newly elected governor will face his share of challenges.

“He’ll still have a Republican-led House and Senate, so they’re going to have to figure out a way to get along. We fight the regulatory issues all the time, code updates, and all of the things that add significant costs,” Iverson told Realtor.com. “So we’re meeting with him even currently, before he’s sworn in, and hoping to deliver on that message of regulatory relief.”

Iverson said he’s looking forward to seeing Sand’s administration work with other governmental bodies to make improvements for Iowans, especially as the two parties come together.

“It’s just going to be a different environment. He sold himself during the campaign on, 'We should all be able to get along, let’s figure this out,’ and I think we’re going to hold him to that,” said Iverson. “I’m looking forward to that part, just seeing what actually happens and how it works. It’s never easy.”

Texas saw a major upset on Tuesday, when the state elected a Democratic governor for the first time since 1991.

Gina Hinojosa, who will make history as Texas’ first Latina elected governor, denied incumbent Republican Gov. Greg Abbott a fourth term with her 7-point victory.

Texas is a consistent front-runner in the Realtor.com state report cards, coming in at No. 4 in 2026. The Lone Star State received a score of 71 out of 100 for a grade of A-.

Its success in the rankings is due to Texas being the nation’s largest homebuilder, accounting for 14.6% of all building permits issued in the country. This far outpaces any other state and is well above the state’s 9.3% population share.

The new-construction premium in Texas is only 7.6%, indicating that builders are adding affordable and competitively priced homes to the state’s inventory.

However, the state struggles slightly with affordability, with the median listing price of $364,749 taking up 32.5% of the state’s median income, giving it a Realtor Affordability Score of 0.66.

Housing has long been a priority of Hinojosa, who has been fighting against multinational corporations and predatory landlords since she was elected to the Texas House of Representatives in 2017. In 2025, she authored a bill that would prevent institutional investors from snapping up more than 10 single-family homes.

As governor, she intends to keep fighting corporate investors and price gouging that keep affordable housing and homeownership out of reach of Texans, according to her campaign.

The Texas Association of Builders feels positive about Hinojosa’s incoming administration and continued bipartisan efforts toward housing affordability in the state.

“She served on the Land and Resource Management Committee in the House this session and was very involved in a lot of the housing discussions and legislation on density, the need for more housing, and increasing housing supply, and was supportive of a number of these efforts during session,” TAB CEO Scott Norman told Realtor.com. “She was campaigning on some of these affordability issues on housing, so we’re excited to work with her and her new team.”

Norman also noted that the Texas Legislature has shown bipartisan efforts to rebuild its skilled workforce and expand training programs on every level, from public schools to trade schools and higher-education institutions.

“To get more people into these jobs that are AI-proof and that are lifetime careers that people can make a great living and own their own business, that’s something that has been very bipartisan,” said Norman.

This week, Democrat Keisha Lance Bottoms made history when she was elected Georgia’s first Black governor and the nation’s first-ever Black female governor.

The former Atlanta mayor captured 54% of the vote against her GOP opponent, Rick Jackson, and will take over from term-limited Republican Brian Kemp.

In the state Affordability & Homebuilding Report Card from 2026, Georgia ranked No. 12, receiving a B for its score of 63 out of 100.

Georgia’s affordability comes in far behind Iowa, with its considerably higher median listing price of $392,112 and slightly lower median income of $75,118. The state comes in with a Realtor Affordability Score of 0.68.

It’s homebuilding, however, is far more significant. Georgia accounts for 4.2% of the country’s building permits, behind only Texas, Florida, California, and North Carolina. The share of permits outpaces Georgia’s share of the nation’s population, which is 3.31%.

During her time as Atlanta mayor, Bottoms raised more than $700 million and helped build or preserve more than 7,000 affordable housing units, according to her campaign. She also introduced the City of Atlanta Housing Affordability Action Plan, which included a road map for increasing housing inventory.

As governor, she plans to create a Georgia Housing Fast Track initiative to further streamline permitting and establish an affordable housing trust fund to assist in the development of housing. Additionally, her platform includes expanding down payment and renter assistance programs throughout the state.

Democrat Amy Acton is the first woman to be elected governor of Ohio, beating Republican Vivek Ramaswamy by 8 points. She will replace term-limited Republican Gov. Mike DeWine.

Ohio earned the No. 15 spot on the 2026 state affordability report card. It received a C+ for its score of 59.4 out of 100.

The state comes in with a healthy Realtor Affordability Score of 0.88, on par with the leading state of Indiana’s 0.89. Its median listing price of $277,348 is reasonable compared to its median income of $70,196.

Of all building permits in the U.S., Ohio accounts for 2.32% of them, slightly behind its share of the nation’s population, which is 3.48%.

The real issue for affordability in Ohio is the 84.6% premium on newly constructed homes compared to existing homes, which indicates that builders are catering to a more expensive market, rather than adding affordable homes to the state’s inventory.

Like the candidates above, Acton’s housing platform centers on removing red tape and streamlining the permitting process to make both new building and the renovation of older properties easier. She also plans to expand the Welcome Home Ohio program, created by DeWine, which provides grants and tax cuts for rehabbing older homes. She wants an expansion to encourage more new construction as well.

Placing restrictions on private equity firms and corporations buying up homes and providing support for first-time buyers are also among Acton’s housing plan, according to Columbus Underground.

The outlier on this list is Nevada. While the rest of the states rank in the top 15 states for affordability on the state report cards, Nevada comes in at No. 34 with a grade of C- for its score of 45.8 points out of 100.

Nevada accounts for 1.3% of the country’s total permits, slightly above its population share of 0.96%. Newly constructed homes come with an 18.5% premium compared to existing homes, which is not outlandish for this list.

The real struggle for Nevada is affordability. The state’s median income is $74,855, on par with No. 2 Iowa, but its median listing prices tower above at $491,936 for a Realtor Affordability Score of just 0.52.

Democrat Aaron D. Ford, the incoming governor, has his work cut out for him in the homebuilding sector.

The former Nevada attorney general held an 8-point victory over Republican incumbent Joe Lombardo on Tuesday  to become the state’s first Black governor.

Ford has laid out a number of pathways through which he intends to make housing more affordable to Nevada residents. Removing red tape and delays in permitting, developing vacant and underused land in the Las Vegas Valley, and expanding support for first-time buyers are among his top priorities.

Ford has also expressed that he will push back on recent tariffs imposed by the Trump administration that have made homes more expensive to build and purchase.

“Nevada families are being forced to absorb higher costs caused by tariffs on essentials like aluminum, steel, lumber, and even basic fixtures like bathroom vanities,” said Ford in this “Affordable Nevada” plan. “A $1,000 increase in the price of a new home pushes an estimated 115,000 households out of reach of homeownership—a price spike working families cannot afford.”

Allaire Conte contributed to this report.

Skyways Starts Production of Its Autonomous Skyreach Cargo Aircraft

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Skyways announced Skyreach, its production autonomous vertical-takeoff-and-landing cargo aircraft, on November 9, 2026, and said manufacturing had started in Austin, Texas. Flights for existing defense and commercial customers are scheduled to begin in December 2026, with the aircraft designed to fly more than 1,300 miles with 10 pounds of cargo.

Originally developed as V3 Block 2, Skyreach is the production platform behind a $37 million STRATFI award from AFWERX, the Department of the Air Force’s innovation arm, in June 2025. The award funded V3’s transition from prototype toward full-rate production. Since then, Skyways has finalized the production design and advanced flight-qualification testing. New customers can reserve capacity.

The Skyreach specification sheet ties that range to a light load: more than 1,300 miles with 10 pounds of cargo, compared with more than 780 miles with 50 pounds. Maximum endurance is listed above 18 hours. The range figures assume level cruise at 70 knots true airspeed, a 2,000-foot density altitude, no wind and a 45-minute fuel reserve.

Cargo and fuel trade off within a 360-pound maximum gross takeoff weight, so the longest-range figure does not describe a maximum-payload flight. Skyways lists useful load, covering fuel and payload, at more than 100 pounds, an 86-pound fuel capacity and a 7-cubic-foot cargo bay.

The earlier award record supplies a baseline: V2 had a 30-pound useful load and a 500-mile maximum range, while the V3 program used the same autonomy stack. Skyreach therefore extends an existing aircraft and software line toward heavier loads and longer missions.

The Skyreach product page describes eight electric motors for vertical lift and a two-stroke heavy-fuel cruise engine with an integrated generator. The generator recharges the lift battery in flight and powers avionics, sensors and payloads, allowing refueling without ground-side battery charging or swaps. Fuel compatibility covers Jet A, Jet A-1, JP-5 and JP-8.

For missions, Skyways lists a bay that can be reconfigured between flights for standard cargo, temperature-controlled blood, plasma and biologics, radar or optical sensors, and communications equipment, with up to 2.5 kilowatts of bay power. Its top-loading, bottom-release layout supports autonomous airdrops and tethered cargo delivery. The design is also intended for shipboard and austere environments, winds up to 40 knots and temperatures from minus 5 to 120 degrees Fahrenheit.

Skyways describes onboard autonomy as handling flight navigation, mission execution and emergency maneuvers. SkyNav Mission Control covers route planning, flight execution and live supervision of aircraft, hubs and operators, presenting a one-operator-to-many-aircraft model in which direct routes can be repeated across a fleet.

Chief Executive Charles Acknin said the company aims to deploy more than 1,000 autonomous aircraft for defense and commercial customers within five years, with Skyways also estimating Skyreach’s cost at roughly one-tenth that of a helicopter.

Production is starting while development continues. The specification sheet labels its numbers “design specifications,” says Skyreach is in flight test and cautions that the figures are subject to change. The production design is finalized, but the performance figures remain provisional as customer flights approach.

The initial customer flights make repeatable service the next test: Skyreach’s range-payload tradeoffs, rapid-refueling design and one-to-many supervision need to hold up in operations to support the fleet scale Skyways is targeting.
